The design driver is that the answer changes through the day, so the screen leads with a **"now" card** (period, section, room) whose single primary action is *Mark this register*, pre-scoped — today that takes four navigations, on a phone, mid-lesson. **Needs no new backend**: `/timetable/mine`, `/notifications`, `/staff-attendance/mine/check-in` and the closure notice all exist, three of them shipped this week. Phased M0 shell (bottom tab bar under 720px, all roles) → M1 home → M2 register on a phone → M3 narrow the rest → M4 PWA. Three decisions in §7. **Offline marking is explicitly out** — a silent sync conflict on a register that feeds pay is worse than waiting for signal.
- [x] **`next build` on Windows — diagnosed 2026-08-09, nothing to fix.** ⚠️ **This entry replaces an alarming one I wrote and then disproved; the correction is the useful part.**
  - **What I claimed:** `next build` fails with `PageNotFoundError: /_document`, the web app is outside the build gate, and the web image cannot be produced — blocking the Coolify deploy.
  - **What is actually true, all three points corrected:** (1) the `/_document` error came from a `.next` I had corrupted by deleting `.next/types` under a running dev server — on a clean tree the build compiles, typechecks and generates all 41 pages; (2) `apps/web` **is** gated — `.github/workflows/ci.yml` has a dedicated `web` job on ubuntu-latest running typecheck → lint → **build**, and the root `pnpm build` omits it *by design* because the web app is a separate pnpm project with its own lockfile; (3) the web image **builds fine** — verified by actually building it.
  - **The only real symptom:** on **Windows**, `next build` dies at the very last step — `writeStandaloneDirectory` → `copyTracedFiles` → `EPERM: symlink`. `output: 'standalone'` recreates symlinks, and Windows needs Developer Mode or an elevated shell to make them. Turn on Developer Mode, or accept that the standalone step is verified in CI and Docker instead. **Nothing about the repo is wrong.**
  - **My own error worth remembering:** I built the image with the repo root as context, so `pnpm build` resolved to the *root* script (`nest build api && nest build worker`) and produced 274 webpack errors inside the web image. The Dockerfile's own header says the context is `apps/web`; `docker build -t school-web apps/web` succeeds. Read the header before concluding the file is broken.

- [x] **Dev proxy followed one tenant, not the URL — FIXED 2026-08-09.** `next.config.mjs` pinned `/api/*` to a single origin decided at server start (`demo.localhost:4000`), so **every API call went to `demo` whatever subdomain the browser was on**. Signing in as another school's teacher returned *"Invalid credentials"* — the password was right, it was being checked against a school that had never heard of them, and nothing in the error said so. It cost me two separate debugging sessions in one day, and the only workaround was a second Next server per tenant. Now a `has: [{ type: 'host' }]` rewrite captures the subdomain and interpolates it into the destination, so `falcon.localhost:3001` reaches `falcon` — same as production. An explicit `NEXT_API_ORIGIN` still wins (CI, e2e and remote-API work depend on pinning it); bare `localhost` falls back to `demo`.
  - **Proved with one server and two tenants:** `demo` host + demo's owner → 200; the other tenant's host + demo's owner → **401**; that tenant's host + its own owner → 200; bare `localhost` → 200 via the fallback. Before the fix the middle two were 200 and 401 — exactly inverted.
  - ⚠️ **The config was only half the cause.** `apps/web/.env.local` (untracked, so invisible to git) also set `NEXT_API_ORIGIN=http://demo.localhost:4000`, and since an explicit value wins, the fix looked like it had done nothing until that was found. The line is now commented out **with the reason written above it**, and the README warns anyone with an older `.env.local` to do the same — a fix defeated by a file git cannot see is one the next person will re-report as a bug.
- [x] **Cover — when a teacher is away, who takes the class. COMPLETE 2026-08-11 (C0–C4).** See [[Cover Plan]]. **The gap is one sentence: the person actually standing in the room cannot mark the register.** `assertCanMark` requires a `TeacherAssignment`, so a substitute gets *"You are not assigned to this section"* and only an admin can close it. The absent teacher is handled correctly — attendance, leave→ON_LEAVE correction, pay — but their periods are unowned and no substitute concept exists anywhere in the schema or API. Design thesis: **staff attendance already knows who is away and the timetable knows what they were teaching, so "what needs cover" is derived, not typed** — the office only picks a name, from a list that puts teachers who are present and free that period first. ⚠️ **Creating cover is a permission grant** (write access to another class's register, backdatable), so it is audited and refused into an APPROVED payroll month. Must work with **no timetable**, since every school has zero slots today. Phased C0 grant → C1 office screen → C2 the covering teacher's surfaces → C3 free-teacher suggestions → C4 tests. Three decisions in §8.
  - **C0 shipped 2026-08-10 and is a complete feature on its own** — `cover_assignments`, `POST`/`DELETE`/`GET ?date=`, the `/cover` three-field screen, three matrix rows, 18 e2e cases. *(The first draft made C0 backend-only with the screen in C1; that would have shipped an increment nobody could use. Corrected before building.)*
  - **`assertCanMark` gained a `date` parameter.** It never had one, because a `TeacherAssignment` is not dated — "may this person mark 9-A?" had a date-free answer. Cover is dated by nature, so the authorization question itself changed shape.
  - ⚠️ **The obvious `@@unique([sectionId, date, periodNo])` cannot work.** `period_no` is NULL for whole-day cover and **Postgres treats NULLs as distinct**, so two whole-day covers on one class would both insert and two people would silently hold the register. Two partial indexes instead (`WHERE period_no IS NOT NULL` / `IS NULL`). This is the *same* NULL behaviour the fee module deliberately relies on for `fee_invoices.psid` — a feature there, a hole here. The duplicate test says so in a comment so nobody "simplifies" it back.
  - **Proven non-vacuous by breaking the code under the tests:** with the date ignored, 1 fails; with the cover branch removed from `assertCanMark`, 2 fail. The list test asserts the *other* day is empty, so a `list` that ignored its date cannot pass.
  - **Verified live on demo:** recorded *9th-A · all day — Ayesha Khan · instead of Haris Ali · Sick leave*; a second cover for the same class returned **"Ayesha Khan is already covering 9th-A that day"**; changing the day emptied the list; Remove took the substitute's right back. Demo left clean.
  - **Still open, deliberately:** `REGISTER_UNMARKED` and the head's unmarked count still name the *absent* teacher rather than the cover (§6a) — one filter each, landing with C2.
  - ⚠️ **Found while doing it:** the demo tenant carries ~110 leftover `Cls<epoch-ms>` classes from Playwright runs, swamping every class dropdown in the app. Same leak as the `admin-<timestamp>` tenants fixed 2026-08-07, one level down: the teardown removes leaked *tenants*, not fixtures created inside the shared `demo` tenant. Filed as its own task.
  - **C1 shipped the same day** — `GET /cover/away?date=` and `POST /cover/range`, the *Away today* panel with click-to-fill, an optional **Until** field, two more matrix rows, 9 more e2e cases (19 in the file).
    - **Approved leave is read alongside the staff register, and that is what makes the list non-empty.** Leave is approved days ahead; the register is marked at 08:00; and a school that keeps no staff register still approves leave. A list built only from the register is blank exactly when the office looks — before school. The register **overrules** the leave where both speak: someone who came in anyway is not away.
    - **`staffRegisterMarked` travels with the answer.** An empty list means two different things — *nobody is away* and *nobody has told us yet* — and the screen says which. Same principle as attendance coverage travelling with the percentage.
    - **Campus scoping goes through the sections, not the person.** `StaffProfile` has no campus; a teacher's campus is decided by what they teach.
    - **A range skips rather than fails** (`{ created, skipped }`, §25.3's shape) and is **capped at 31 days** — a date picker held down produces a year, and a year of cover is a reassignment. The cap also means `from`/`to` name every month the grant touches, so the payroll-freeze check on those two is complete.
    - **Probed three ways:** ignoring approved leave fails 1; ignoring the school calendar in a range fails 1; dropping section dedupe fails 1.
    - **Verified live:** marked *Haris Ali* absent → his five classes listed as *needs cover*; clicking **9th-A** filled the class and *instead of*, leaving one name to choose; saving flipped the chip to **9th-A · Ayesha Khan** and dropped the count to 4; a 10th→12th range answered *"3 days covered."* and the row was really there on the 12th. Demo left clean, staff-attendance rows included.
  - **C2 shipped 2026-08-11** — `GET /cover/mine`, `COVERING_TODAY`/`COVERED_TODAY`, the cover card on `/home` and `/my-timetable`, and **§6a**. 4 more e2e cases (23 in the file).
    - **C2 is the phase that takes something away**, not just adds: once 9-A is covered it stops being the absent teacher's register that day. `registerUnmarked` now asks `sectionsResponsibleFor` (own sections − covered by others + covering for others), and `unmarkedToday` rows carry `coveredBy`. The test asserts the count **moves** rather than the notice vanishing — Nadia keeps a reminder for 9-B, Fatima gets one only because of cover.
    - ⚠️ **What makes a register somebody else's is that somebody else HOLDS it** — the query keys on `coveringStaffId`, never `absentStaffId`. That field is optional and the office often records only who is covering; keying on it would have made re-attribution work only when the form was filled in fully.
    - **Both notices are derived, so both vanish when the cover is removed** — asserted. A stored notice telling someone to take a class reassigned an hour ago is the failure the no-table rule exists to prevent.
    - **`@Roles` moved from the `CoverController` class to its methods.** `/cover/mine` is self-scoped and must be open to the teacher it is about, and `RolesGuard` uses `getAllAndOverride([handler, class])` — a handler with no metadata inherits the class's, so a class-level decorator cannot be opted out of. Safe because the matrix has a row per admin route: conformance still passes 504/504 after the move.
    - ⚠️ **A real bug found in code C2 only happened to read.** `unmarkedToday` and `registerUnmarked` counted expected students with `startedAt: { lte: <midnight> }` — but **`startedAt` carries a time**, so a student enrolled *earlier the same day* was excluded, while marking and the coverage strip both normalise (`startOfDay(a) <= startOfDay(b)`). On their admission day a student's register could be **marked but never reported as outstanding**. Fixed by comparing against the start of tomorrow. **Fourth time one question has had two implementations here and they disagreed.**
    - **Probed four ways:** responsibility not moving fails 1; nobody told fails 2; the head's list forgetting the cover fails 1; reverting the `startedAt` fix fails 1.
    - **Verified live on demo** (Haris away, Ayesha covering 10th-A, a class she does not teach): the head's chip read **"10th A · Ayesha Khan covering →"**; her home card read **COVERING · ALL DAY / 10th-A / For Haris Ali · Sick**; her bell said *"You are covering 10th-A today for Haris Ali."*; **My Timetable showed the cover on its EMPTY-week branch** — the one every school hits today, and the branch an early return would have hidden it behind. Her unmarked count read 5 rather than her usual 4, which is §6a moving a class onto her. *Not* browser-verified: the absent teacher's own view — it needed a second password swap, and both directions are asserted by e2e under real teacher sessions. Demo left clean, password hash restored.
    - ⚠️ **A muddied test run, recorded because the conclusion nearly went the other way.** A full integration run came back `2 suites / 14 tests failed` (exams, admissions) with absurd 6-hour suite durations — while I was driving a browser session and `psql` against the same Postgres, across local midnight. Both suites pass 14/14 alone, and a clean full run with nothing else touching the DB is **868/868**. The failure was mine, not the suites'; the reportable fact is that concurrent DB work invalidates a run rather than slowing it.
  - **C3 shipped 2026-08-11** — `GET /cover/suggestions`, the ordered and annotated *Covered by* dropdown, a matrix row, 5 more e2e cases (28 in the file).
    - **Four statuses, each meaning only what the data supports:** `FREE` (in school **and** the timetable positively says nothing else that period) · `IN_SCHOOL` (in school, no timetable to check) · `BUSY` (names the class) · `AWAY` (names why). ⚠️ **`timetableKnown` is load-bearing**: `timetable_slots` is empty in every school, so `FREE` would otherwise be claimed for the whole staff body on no evidence, and an office that trusts it double-books somebody.
    - **Nobody is hidden.** An administrator may know something the register does not — someone who came in late and unmarked — so the list **orders and explains rather than forbids**. Only non-teachers and people whose `leftAt` has passed are excluded.
    - ⚠️ **A probe found nothing to break, and that was the finding.** Breaking "the register overrules approved leave" inside `suggestions` failed **zero** tests: C1's `away()` and C3's `suggestions()` had each grown their own copy of that precedence and only C1's was covered. Now one `whoIsAway()` — plus a C3-side test, because **a shared helper with one caller under test is the same hole wearing a better name**. Re-probed: it now fails both callers.
    - **Probed three ways:** claiming `FREE` without checking a timetable exists fails 1; `dayOfWeek` left as `getUTCDay()` (0 = Sunday, off by one) fails 1; the leave precedence fails 2.
    - **Verified live:** with Haris absent, picking 10th-A ended the dropdown with **"Haris Ali — marked absent"** — last but still selectable — and read *"in school today"* for everyone else with **nobody "free"**, which is the honest answer for a school with no timetable.
  - **C4 closed the plan 2026-08-11** — the four cases C0–C3 did not need. **32 e2e cases in the file.**
    - **Cover is a day's attendance, not a teaching relationship:** the covering teacher **cannot enter exam marks** for the class she covered. That was already true by construction (marks entry asks `TeacherAssignment` and never consults cover); the case exists so a future *"we could reuse `coversSectionOn` here"* cannot widen the grant silently.
    - **The campus boundary was finally watched failing.** The rule had been written three times across C1–C3 and never once observed to work, because every fixture had one campus. C4 adds a second with a `CAMPUS_ADMIN` bound to the first: arranging cover across → 403; the list shows **1 of 2** (both sides populated, so the count is the assertion); suggestions across → 403; `/cover/away` shows 2 of her 3 classes.
    - ⚠️ **The first version of that last case could not fail.** Deleting the campus filter from `away()` broke nothing — the away teacher had no classes at the far campus, so there was nothing for a leak to expose. The fixture now assigns her at **both**, the owner is asserted to see 3 while the campus admin sees exactly 2, and the probe fails as it should. **A boundary test needs something on the wrong side of the boundary** — same shape as the TT4 timetable-scoping rewrite. It also asserts she is still *listed*: a filter that drops the colleague is as wrong as one that leaks the campus, and checking only the leak direction would miss it.
    - **Probed three ways:** marks honouring cover fails 1; the list dropping its campus filter fails 1; `away()` dropping its filter fails 1 **after** the fixture was fixed and **0 before**.
    - ⚠️ **Found while writing these, filed separately:** any route taking `:id` answers a **malformed id with a 500**, not a 404 — the value reaches Prisma unvalidated (`Inconsistent column data: Error creating UUID`). Surfaced as `POST /api/v1/exams/undefined/...` when an unchecked fixture step left `undefined` in the URL, and it cost real time: the failure read as *the feature is broken* when only the fixture was. `ParseUUIDPipe` is the fix. ✅ **FIXED 2026-08-17** — see the entry below.
  - **What the plan cost, and what it bought.** Five phases in two days: `cover_assignments` + two partial unique indexes + one migration; six endpoints; one new screen and four existing surfaces changed; 32 e2e cases; and **three real bugs fixed in code cover only happened to read** — the `startedAt` day comparison, the duplicated away/leave precedence, and the CI typecheck gate that had been red on `main`. The gap it closed is still one sentence: **the person actually standing in the room can mark the register.**
  - **Gates:** unit 57 · integration **882/882 across 36 suites** · isolation 7 · both lints · root + Playwright typecheck · api+worker build · web tsc + lint · migration safety (14 objects) · RLS coverage.
- [x] **CI's typecheck step was red on `main` — FIXED 2026-08-10.** ⚠️ **A merge gate that has been failing is worse than no gate**: every "gates green" claim in this file, mine included, was made against a *local* list (lint · `tsc -p apps/api/tsconfig.app.json` · builds · tests) that never ran the command CI actually runs. `npx tsc -p tsconfig.json --noEmit` — the exact CI step — produced **10 errors on a pristine `main` worktree**. Found incidentally while typechecking the Cover C1 branch.
  - **Seven were one root cause:** Playwright specs run *inside a browser page* (`page.evaluate`) and legitimately use `document`, `fetch`, `Response`, `File`, but the root project is Node with `lib: ["ES2022"]` and no DOM — deliberately, so API code cannot reach a browser global by accident. Without DOM, `Response` is unknown, so `r.json()` degrades to `{}` and every property access on a fetch result errors. `test/e2e` now has its **own** tsconfig with `lib: ["ES2022","DOM"]`, is excluded from the root project, and gets its **own CI step** — without that step the split would just be a place for errors to hide.
  - **Three were `MatrixRole`:** three rows name `HR_MANAGER` as an admitted role while the union did not contain it. Added to the union, **not** to `MATRIX_ROLES` — nothing has ever exercised an HR_MANAGER session, and listing it as tested without seeding it is the exact drift §23 was softened for. Doing it properly (every row's deny side true of HR_MANAGER) is real work, filed rather than faked.
  - ⚠️ **The first version of the fix passed while checking nothing.** `exclude` is inherited from the base config and its paths resolve against the **base** directory, so the root's `"test/e2e"` excluded every file the new config included — and `tsc -p` reported a clean run over **zero files**. Caught only because eslint then refused to parse those same files ("not found in any of the provided project(s)"). `--listFiles` now confirms 24. **A gate that checks nothing passes loudly**, which is the same lesson as a test that cannot fail.
  - Also widened eslint's `parserOptions.project` to both tsconfigs: a file in no project is a file eslint silently refuses to parse, which is a hole, not a smaller lint run.
- [x] **Teacher app shell — COMPLETE 2026-08-11 (T0–T4).** See [[Teacher App Shell Plan]]. Raised by the operator: the teacher home "is not like a default mobile screen — there are tabs, not menus". **Checked live before planning: the tab bar is NOT broken** — at 375px it renders four tabs pinned to the bottom with the sidebar hidden, exactly as M0 says. Three other things are, and the report came from a screenshot cropped above the bar.
  - ⚠️ **On desktop a teacher's `/home` is an ORPHAN.** At 1440px the sidebar has 8 links and none is `/home` — it is `hidden: true` so the tab bar can own it, which is right on a phone and strands it above 720px. A teacher lands on Home, clicks anything, and **cannot get back except with the browser's back button.** The home screen four phases went into is unreachable on a laptop after one click.
  - **The two navigations disagree**: 4 items vs 8 in 3 admin groups; "Week" vs "My Timetable"; no Home or Me on desktop; "MY PORTAL" is an admin's word for a teacher's own things.
  - ⚠️ **A teacher who wears a second hat never sees the teacher shell at all.** `usesMobileShell` keys off `primaryRole`, which returns the first match in `ROLE_INFO` order — **TEACHER is 7th**, behind ADMISSION_CONTROLLER and HR_MANAGER among others. Demo's own `ayeshakhan@gmail.com` is `TEACHER, ADMISSION_CONTROLLER` and gets the Admission Portal shell: no tab bar, no Home, **on a phone as well**. In a small Pakistani private school the teacher who also does one admin job is normal staffing, not an edge case.
  - **The home screen contradicts itself in the state every school is in:** headline *"A register needs marking"* over body *"No timetable has been set for you yet"* and a generic *Open attendance*. The system knows which registers (`unmarked-today` ∩ `my-classes`) and declines to say. M1's "one tap" degrades to opening the screen cold — the exact problem M1 existed to solve.
  - **Thesis: role decides the shell, width decides only the layout.** One list of destinations rendered as a bottom bar ≤720px and a teacher sidebar above it. **Operator decisions taken:** app shell at every width; scope = navigation + the home screen; and for T1, **the teacher shell wins unless the person also holds OWNER_ADMIN or CAMPUS_ADMIN** (so it beats accountant, admission and HR, but never a school-wide administrator).
  - **T0 SHIPPED 2026-08-11.** `TeacherSidebarNav` beside `TeacherTabs`, both reading the same `tabsFor()` list; the shell renders it instead of the grouped admin nav for a teacher. A teacher at 1440px now gets **Home · Attendance · Week · Me** and can return to Home from every screen; at 375px the bottom bar is unchanged; an owner is untouched (re-checked: 19 links, 6 groups, no tab bar in the DOM). `test/e2e/teacher-shell.spec.ts`, 2 cases — probed by reverting the shell, which fails the first.
    - **The desktop sidebar is four items, not ten, deliberately.** Hoisting the secondary screens up "because a laptop has room" would invent a second information architecture for the same person; the phone's structure IS the product's structure. They stay behind **Me**. Reversible in one line if a teacher actually complains.
    - **The second case asserts the OTHER direction** — an owner still gets the grouped admin nav and no tab bar. A change scoped to one role is exactly the kind that quietly widens, and a suite that only ever looks at the teacher would never see it.
    - **T1 SHIPPED the same day.** `usesMobileShell` → **`usesTeacherShell`**, with the rule written down instead of inherited: *hold TEACHER and you get the app, unless you also administer the school* (`PLATFORM_ADMIN` / `OWNER_ADMIN` / `CAMPUS_ADMIN`). It used to be `primaryRole(roles)?.role === 'TEACHER'` — the answer fell out of **the ordering of a list written for a different purpose**, in which TEACHER sits 7th, so every teacher who also kept the books, ran admissions or did HR was silently excluded from the app built for them. **A rule that matters should be written down, not implied by an array's order.**
      - `landingPath` and `panelLabel` now follow the same function, so the shell, the landing screen and the name in the corner cannot disagree. ⚠️ **Behaviour change for existing accounts:** an accountant-who-teaches who opens on `/dashboard` today opens on `/home` after this — decided, not accidental.
      - **Last tab renamed Me → More**, because for a dual-role teacher it holds Dashboard, Fees, Payment submissions and Reports — a whole second job under a person icon.
      - **Probed:** reverting to the old `primaryRole` rule fails the new dual-role case. That case also asserts the accounting job is still reachable under More — without it, "give the teacher an app" could quietly have meant "take the other half of their work away".
      - ⚠️ **Two test-quality fixes, both about failures that lie.** `waitForURL('**/home')` spent the full 30s budget and reported only "timeout"; it now fails in ~5s with **"Expected /home, Received .../dashboard"** — when that test breaks the landing page *is* the question. And `apiSetupDelete`/`ctx.close()` threw during teardown after a failed test, so Playwright reported *those* instead of the real assertion; both are now best-effort. **Cleanup must never be the loudest thing in a failure.**
    - **T2 SHIPPED the same day — the home screen names the register.** *"A register needs marking / No timetable has been set for you yet / Open attendance"* became **"Mark 9th-A / 4 students / Mark this register"**, linking straight to that section. A headline promising a task, a body withdrawing it, and a button that opened a screen cold — replaced by the answer.
      - ⚠️ **The plan's "no new endpoints" was wrong, and saying so is the point.** `/attendance/unmarked-today` is admin-only and the teacher-callable `/attendance/coverage` is per-section, so naming four registers meant four requests on a phone. But the *logic* was not new: **`registerUnmarked` already computed this list and reduced it to a count** — the names were thrown away one line before they were needed. One `myUnmarkedToday()` now, two consumers: the bell collapses it to a number, the home names the classes. Same discipline as `whoIsAway()` in Cover C3.
      - ⚠️ **Two bugs I wrote, both caught in a browser, neither catchable by the tests I had.** (1) The good-news branch was **unreachable** — I derived "has classes" from `sections.length`, which holds only the *unmarked* ones, so a teacher who had just finished marking was told *"No timetable has been set for you yet."* Fixed by returning **`responsible`**, the count before filtering: *"you have no classes"* and *"all of yours are done"* otherwise arrive identically as `[]`, and only one is good news. (2) It would have **lied before the mark-by time** — gating the card on `due` (copied from the bell) emptied the list early and hit the same branch, claiming everything was marked at 08:30. The card is now deliberately **not** `due`-gated: the deadline stops a *warning* firing during the lesson it is about, and this is not a warning, it is the answer to "what am I doing now".
      - **Probed:** dropping cover-awareness fails 2; making `responsible` mirror the unmarked count fails 1. **Verified live:** the pre-scoped link loaded the right roster with 4 rows and no picking; saving flipped the card to *Nothing to mark*.
      - ⚠️ **Found while doing it:** `POST /teacher-assignments` accepts a teacher at one campus being assigned a section at **another**; the home then says *Mark 9th-A* for a register the API refuses with *"Resource belongs to another campus"*. Always possible — T2 made it visible by naming the class and linking to it. Filed, and it needs checking for `/timetable/slots` and `/cover` too.
    - **T3 SHIPPED the same day — the teacher's home at desktop width.** Measured first at 1440×900: the "now" card was **1164px wide with 474px of empty viewport beneath it**. Now **640px** in a two-column grid above 1024px — a 26px headline across 1164px is about ninety characters, well past a readable measure. **The 474px of empty space is left alone deliberately:** a teacher with three registers and no timetable has a short day, and filling it would be padding. What was wrong was the stretching, not the emptiness.
      - **The second column carries something real:** `restUnmarked` had been computed since T2 and rendered **nowhere** — the card counted them ("2 more registers after this") so a teacher could see more existed but not what they were. Now a list, one tap each. ⚠️ **Shown at both widths, not desktop-only** — extra cards on the desktop would be a second product for the same person, which is the thing this plan exists to undo. **Width changes the arrangement, never the content.**
      - **The dual-role screens needed nothing.** T1 flagged that a teacher-plus-accountant opens `/fees` inside the teacher shell; checked by construction — `.shell` is `220px 1fr` for both shells and `.content` is untouched, so the content column is identical. Concern unfounded.
      - ⚠️ ~~**I broke the Playwright suite and then fixed it.** T1's dual-role case was a sixth form login against the 5/IP/15min limit, so a full run started failing on whichever spec logged in last. Fixed by merging the two teacher-session cases into one.~~ **WRONG — corrected 2026-08-11.** The limiter was off (`RATE_LIMIT_ENABLED=false` in `.env`), the run has always made ~13 logins rather than 5, and the merge has been undone: `teacher-shell` is two cases again. See the correction entry in the cross-cutting backlog.
      - **Probed:** deleting the `@media (min-width: 1024px)` rule fails the layout assertion with *Expected 2, Received 1*.
    - **T4 closed the plan** — every teacher surface checked at 375 / 768 / 1440. **768 is the width nobody had ever looked at**: above the 720px shell breakpoint so there is a sidebar and no tab bar, below the 1024px home breakpoint so the home is one column in 578px. `/home` 542px card, `/attendance` 540px table with **67×44** mark buttons, `/me-more` headed **"More"**, `/my-timetable` honest empty state — **zero horizontal overflow on all of them, and nothing needed fixing.** Recorded anyway: "we never looked at that width" is how the 1164px card survived four phases, and a checked width with nothing wrong is worth as much in the record as a broken one.
    - **What the plan cost and bought.** Five phases in one afternoon, off one screenshot and *"there are tabs, not menus"* — and **the reported problem was not the real one**: the tab bar worked, the screenshot was cropped above it. Checking before planning turned a non-bug into four real ones (orphaned `/home` on desktop · dual-role teachers never seeing the app at all · a home screen contradicting itself · cards never reviewed above 720px), plus three found in passing and filed, plus two I wrote in T2 and caught in a browser. **The thesis held:** every attempt to make the desktop *different* was rejected on it.
    - ⚠️ **A stale `dist/tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` makes the root typecheck report an error that does not exist.** `npx tsc -p tsconfig.json --noEmit` claimed `TS2321: Excessive stack depth` in `libs/database/src/tenant-prisma.service.ts` — a file nobody had touched — and it survived stashing every local change. **`rm dist/tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` clears it.** CI always runs fresh so it never sees this, which is exactly what makes it expensive: it only ever wastes a developer's time, and it looks like a real failure in a file you cannot explain.
    - ⚠️ ~~**The suite now makes exactly 5 form logins per run against the §29 limit of 5/IP/15min — zero headroom.** A clean run passes; running one spec twice during development and then the full suite does not, and **the symptom is a 30-second navigation timeout that reads as a broken feature**, not a rate-limit error. That is exactly what happened: `staff-attendance` failed in a full run, passed in isolation, passed again once the window drained. Filed as its own task; the fix is fewer logins (a shared teacher `storageState` setup project), **not a higher limit** — the limit is real production behaviour worth testing against. *The tracker already recorded this trap from the other side ("the earlier per-test login design blew the limit at 8 logins/run"); it has now been re-approached and re-learned.*~~ ⚠️ **WRONG — the limiter was switched off here the whole time. See the correction entry in the cross-cutting backlog below.**
- [x] **Student transfer — COMPLETE 2026-08-11 (X0–X3).** See [[Student Transfer Plan]]. Raised by the operator: *"can a student be moved from one class to another?"* **The backend has been able to since M2 and nobody can reach it** — `POST /enrollments/transfer` closes the old enrolment as TRANSFERRED_OUT and opens a new ACTIVE one on the target section (taking `classId` **and `campusId`** from it, so it moves across sections, classes *and* campuses), audits both sides, and has **no UI at all**. That is the **fourth** instance of this shape here, after CSV import, `/my-attendance` and `/my-leaves`.
  - **X0 closed three gaps and uncovered a fourth.** ⚠️ **No campus check** — `EnrollmentService` did not import `assertCampusAccess`, so a campus admin could move a child into or out of another campus. ⚠️ **No capacity check** — admission honours `sectionCapacityMode`, transfer ignored it, so a 41st child could be *moved* into a section a new admission would have been refused from. ⚠️ **No matrix rows** for transfer or promotions.
  - **The capacity rule moved rather than being copied.** It was **private on `StudentsService`**, which is exactly why transfer never had it; now `SetupService.assertSectionHasRoom`, with admission delegating. Same discipline as `whoIsAway()` and `myUnmarkedToday()`. ADVISORY still lets the write through — a school that has not opted into a hard cap is saying its class sizes are guidance — and the test asserts **both** modes, because a capacity test that only ever sees a refusal cannot tell a working rule from a broken endpoint.
  - ⚠️ **Adding a matrix row found a fourth hole: `GET /enrollments` had NO `@Roles` at all**, so any authenticated session — a student, a parent — could enumerate which child is in which class across the school, and it was **not campus-scoped** either. Both closed. **This is the argument for the permission matrix in one incident:** the write beside it was correctly gated the whole time, and *a guarded write does not imply a guarded read* was already a recorded lesson here.
  - ⚠️ **The campus test could only fail in one direction.** The first version had a campus admin move their own student away; deleting the source-end check broke nothing, because the destination check catches that on its own. It now also tests an admin of the **far** campus pulling the child in, where only the source can refuse. **A guard with two halves needs a case per half** — one case finds whichever half runs first and tells you nothing about the other.
  - **Probed:** capacity ignored fails 1; source-end campus check removed fails 1 (0 before the pull-in case existed).
  - **Behaviour changes worth stating:** a campus admin moving students across campuses now gets 403; their `GET /enrollments` returns their campus only; a move into a full section is refused in HARD schools.
  - **X1 SHIPPED the same day — the move is reachable.** A **Move** action on each Students row opens a dialog that says *Currently 8th-B*, lists target sections as `A — 4 of 40 seats`, refuses a full section before the click in HARD schools (warns in ADVISORY), disables the button for the section they are already in, and states **"Only today onwards moves"** — the sentence that stops the history staying behind being reported as data loss. `test/e2e/student-move.spec.ts` covers it.
    - ⚠️ **Correction I made mid-decision:** I told the operator there was no student detail view, having inferred it from the file listing rather than reading the file — `StudentProfile` exists and is rendered in place. Option 1 (a row action beside *Change status*, since a move is a correction and not a sanction) was chosen and still holds, but one of the options they weighed had been described wrongly.
    - **Probed:** dropping the "Only today onwards moves" clause fails the spec. The e2e also asserts the **old section is empty afterwards** — checking only the destination would pass on an endpoint that *copied* an enrolment instead of moving it.
    - ⚠️ **My first probe was malformed JSX** and broke the page compile, so the run failed in the login *setup* projects and proved nothing. **A probe must break the behaviour under test, not the build** — otherwise the red tells you about your edit, not about your test.
    - **Verified live:** moved a real demo student 8th-B → 9th-A and back, watching the "Currently" line and the same-section guard update. Demo left as found.
  - **X2 gave it a second entry point — on the student's PROFILE, not the class workbench the plan named.** ⚠️ The workbench has **no roster**: 277 lines showing a strength count and a *View students* button that routes to the filtered Students list, where Move already lives. Building a roster purely to host the action would have been inventing a screen to justify a plan. The profile is the right home — **someone reading one child's record is exactly who notices they are in the wrong room.** Dialog extracted to `components/move-student-dialog.tsx`, props narrowed to `{ id, fullName }` so a `StudentDetail` fits without a second shape.
    - ⚠️ **My first refresh assertion was vacuous:** `toContainText(sectionName)` where `sectionName` is a **single letter** the card already contained inside *"Status ACTIVE"* — deleting the profile's refetch failed nothing. Now asserts the card **no longer names the section they left**, using the generated `S<timestamp>`. **A generated name is unique; a letter is a substring of half the page.** Re-probed: it fails.
  - **What the plan bought.** Four phases from one question — *"can a student be moved?"* — and the answer was **"yes, and nobody can"**: shipped in M2 with no UI, no campus check, no capacity check and no matrix row, plus a fifth thing the matrix row exposed (ungated `GET /enrollments`). **Two of the gaps were only visible because something else was being written down** — the capacity rule surfaced when it had to be shared, the read leak when the route was finally described in the matrix. *Describing a thing precisely is a way of testing it.*
  - **Gates:** unit 57 · integration **898/898 across 36 suites** · isolation 7 · Playwright **36** · lints · root/e2e/web typechecks · api+worker build.
- [x] **Cross-campus pairs rejected — FIXED 2026-08-11.** `POST /teacher-assignments` accepted a teacher at campus A being linked to a class at campus B; so did `POST /timetable/slots` and `POST /cover`. ⚠️ **Not "no check" — the checks were about the CALLER.** `assertCampusAccess` takes the caller, and an owner is school-wide, so each end was validated against the person clicking and never against the other end. The assignment saved, the teacher's home said *"Mark 9-A"*, and attendance then refused them for being at another campus — the system instructing somebody to do a thing it would block.
  - **One shared rule, three call sites:** `assertSameCampus(personCampus, resourceCampus, label)` in `libs/common/src/authz/campus-scope.ts`. **422, not 403** — nobody's permissions are at fault, the *pair* is invalid. A `null` person campus (an owner who also teaches) is school-wide and allowed anywhere.
  - ⚠️ **Fixing it broke four Cover C4 tests, and that was the fixture's fault.** Their `beforeAll` assigned the away teacher to a section at the far campus so the away-list filter had something to leak — an assignment that only ever succeeded *because of this hole*. Rebuilt with a teacher **of** that campus who is also away: the owner sees two away teachers, the campus admin sees one. **When a fix breaks a fixture, check whether the fixture was exercising the defect before weakening the fix.**
  - ⚠️ **My first probe was a no-op** — `if (a === b) return;` inserted before `if (a !== b) throw` changes nothing, and the 45/45 pass it produced proved nothing. Re-probed by deleting the rule outright: **2 failed**, one per new case (cover, and assignments+timetable).
  - ⚠️ **It contradicted an earlier decision, and the operator reversed that one.** `class-structure.e2e` asserted *"a campus admin sees an assignment on their OWN section even when the teacher sits in another campus"* — written when scoping by the *teacher's* campus hid rows from the admin who owned the section. Right about the scoping, wrong about the premise. **Operator's call: a teacher belongs to one campus and may only teach there.** The case can no longer be constructed (teacher campus now always equals section campus), so it was rewritten to the half that still means something; a second case that used a campus-B teacher on a campus-A section only to test the YEAR filter now uses a second campus-A teacher. **A test that encodes a decision is evidence, not an obstacle.**
  - ⚠️ **I twice blamed this on the environment and was twice wrong.** Docker Desktop was slow to start, and `timeout N docker … 2>&1 | head` swallowed the connection error into empty output, which I read as *"no containers, no images, no volumes — a factory reset"*. Nothing was reset: the containers were four days old with volumes intact. The 2 `class-structure` failures were **real and mine** the whole time. **Check a diagnosis that conveniently absolves your change.**
  - **Gates:** unit 57 · integration **900/900 across 36 suites** · isolation 7 · lints · root/e2e/web typechecks · api+worker build.
- [x] **⚠️ Playwright login-limiter diagnosis was WRONG — corrected 2026-08-11.** Three intermittent failures (`staff-attendance`, `closure-banner`, one setup-project run) were blamed on the §29 login limiter across three commit messages and two brain notes, and T3 "fixed" it by merging two `teacher-shell` cases into one. **The limiter does not fire here at all** — measured 2026-08-11: seven consecutive logins returned 200 with `RATE_LIMIT_ENABLED=false`, **and still 200 with it set to `true`** after an API restart and a Redis flush. ⚠️ **Why is unresolved**: there is no `dotenv` import anywhere, so `.env` may never be read, and the schema defaults the flag to `'true'`. The count was wrong too — `seedClassSectionStudent` logs in as the admission officer and **eight specs call it**, so a run has always made **~13** logins, not 5.
  - **The merge is undone.** `teacher-shell` is two cases again: a plain teacher (shell + layout + Home reachable at both widths) and a dual-role one (lands on `/home`, accounting job under More). There was no login budget to economise against.
  - **The likely real cause:** `next dev` compiles a route on first visit, so whichever spec hits a cold route on a busy machine eats the 30-second budget — fits red-in-a-full-run / green-in-isolation / green-later exactly, and needs no limiter. *(The setup-project failure really was mine: a malformed JSX probe that broke the compile.)*
  - ⚠️ **My replacement claim was ALSO wrong.** I first "corrected" this to *"a fresh clone runs with the limiter ON and fails on its first run"* — then tested it, set the flag to `true`, restarted the API, flushed Redis, and seven logins still passed. **Two wrong explanations in a row for one observation.** Only the measurement is established; the mechanism is open. A shared officer session is still worth doing as **tidiness** (thirteen logins where the design says two), not as a fix for a failure anyone has seen. `seedClassSectionStudent` and both specs carry notes saying exactly that.
  - ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-08-12 — and the "no dotenv import" observation was literally true and completely misleading.** There is no `dotenv` **package** import, because the project ships **its own loader**: `loadDotenv()` in `libs/common/src/config/load-env.ts`, called on the first line of `bootstrap()` in **both** `apps/api/src/main.ts` and `apps/worker/src/main.ts`, with the comment *"dev: populate process.env from .env before config validation"*. So `.env` is read deliberately and by design, and it carries `RATE_LIMIT_ENABLED=false`. *(Separately measured and true but NOT the mechanism here: requiring `@prisma/client` also populates `.env`. Two candidate paths, one of them intended — grep for the behaviour, not the package name.)* `ConfigModule` validates that already-populated `process.env`, `RateLimitGuard.enabled` is false, and `canActivate` returns on its first line — so `consume()` is never called and **no `rl:*` key has ever been written**. That zero-key check is what settles it: the limiter was not failing to deny, it was not running at all.
  - **Both directions measured, on the same build.** Against the dev API (:4000, flag from `.env`): 8 consecutive bad logins → 8 × 401, no 429, zero Redis keys. Against a probe instance started with `RATE_LIMIT_ENABLED=true` (a real environment variable takes precedence over `.env`): attempts 1–5 → 401, **attempts 6–8 → 429**, with `rl:login:ip:*` and `rl:login:email:*` both created. **§29 is implemented correctly and works; it is simply switched off locally.**
  - ⚠️ **The exposure is deployment, not code.** `docker-compose.prod.yml` feeds the API `env_file: .env`, and its `x-app-env` anchor does **not** set `RATE_LIMIT_ENABLED` — so the deployed stack takes the flag from whatever `.env` it is given, and the running container reports `false` today. The Zod schema's `.default('true')` protects only the case where the key is **absent**; a copied dev `.env` makes it present and false. **`.env.example` (94 lines) does not mention the variable at all**, so nobody preparing a production env file is prompted to set it, and nothing fails loudly if they don't — the app simply serves unlimited login attempts.
  - **Fixed by making the unsafe state unbootable.** `validateEnv` refuses to start a production API with the limiter off — verified against the real build, which exits with *"refusing to start: rate limiting is disabled while NODE_ENV=production… Set RATE_LIMIT_ENABLED=true"* — and `docker-compose.prod.yml` now pins `RATE_LIMIT_ENABLED: 'true'` in `x-app-env`, which overrides `env_file`. ⚠️ **That pin is load-bearing, not belt-and-braces:** the prod stack reads the same `.env` as local dev, so without it this change would simply have stopped the stack booting, and "fix it by setting `.env` to true" would have switched the limiter on for the dev API and started failing the e2e suite's ~13 logins. `.env.example` documents the flag with the warning. **5 new unit cases** (2 for the refusal, 3 guarding against an over-broad rule: production-with-it-on, key-absent, and non-production-off), probed by removing the rule — the 2 fail, the 3 still pass.
  - **The earlier "still 200 with the flag set to true" measurement is still unexplained and no longer worth chasing**: `nest start --watch` does not restart on a `.env` change, and there are two API instances on this machine (local dev :4000 and a container on :4001) — either would produce that reading. **What matters is the mechanism, and it is now demonstrated in both directions.**
  - **The lesson:** *a diagnosis that neatly explains a flake, costs nothing to believe, and is never tested is the easiest kind to get wrong* — and the correction is not a better guess, it is writing down only what was measured and marking the rest open. Nobody checked the flag for three days; the first check produced a second wrong answer.
- [x] **⚠️ The e2e suite was writing to the product it tests — FIXED 2026-08-12.** The demo tenant held **185 `Cls<timestamp>` classes against 3 real ones** and **64 fee heads of which 33 were `Tuition <timestamp>`**: the operator's class and fee pickers were mostly test debris. Tolerated for months as harmless because it sat "in a campus nobody looks at" — which stopped being true once Cover and student-Move started listing every class in the school. **Nothing enforced a ceiling because nothing could: the count grew with how often the suite ran, not with how much was built.**
  - **Purged** in one transaction, after a `pg_dump` backup, with the delete order derived from the FK graph and a guard refusing to run if a target still held a non-fixture row: 185 classes → 0, 64 fee heads → 5 (the survivors pinned by real invoice line items), 53 seeded students removed. **3 real classes, 19 real students and the E2E officer untouched.**
  - **Operator's two decisions:** reuse one stable class rather than mint one per run, and purge the existing debris outright.
  - ⚠️ **One shared class broke four specs at once, and every one of them was right to break.** Freshness had been doing the isolation work silently: the exam roster loaded a neighbour's child, the move spec found its old section occupied, and the fee specs read totals and batches left by their neighbours. The shape that works is **one stable class per spec** — nine of them, fixed, plus a per-run `scratch` class for the two specs that mutate structure and delete it again (`classes`, `fee-plan`). **Ask what a test would assert if somebody else had used the fixture first.**
  - **Financial records are not debris, so the design bends around them.** No DELETE exists for an invoice or a batch, and `createBatch` returns early for a month it has already billed — leaving that run's child with no invoice at all. Both fee specs now walk forward to an unbilled month. The teardown mirrors `softDelete` and **refuses to remove a child with payment or certificate history**, so ~2 per run legitimately stay; it is scoped by **campus, not by name**, which the earlier `Student %` filter had used while 22 `Imp One/Two <ts>` siblings piled up behind it.
  - **Three real test bugs surfaced underneath the debris**, each previously masked: `fee-guardian-link` minted its link for **the first unpaid invoice in the whole tenant** — possibly a child it never created — then asserted the page hid that stranger's name; a generate-loop read `.toast.ok` from the *previous* iteration and silently billed **three months in one run**; and `fee-claims` carried a loading race whose own documented fix (`expect(Loading…).toHaveCount(0)`) does not close it, because that also passes *before* the spinner appears. The last only showed up once the tenant was big enough to make the request slow — **the debris was hiding a race, then revealing it.**
  - ⚠️ **Two specs failed because the app said the right thing twice**: "Move earlier" is correctly hidden for a class with no neighbour (the spec had only ever passed because fixture classes gave the first card siblings), and the closure banner now legitimately renders in both the shell and the "Staff today" card.
  - **Verified by running the full suite twice in a row** — the acceptance test for a reusable fixture, since every defect above except the first survived one green run and appeared on the second. Classes (11), sections (15) and fee heads (7) now hold **flat** across runs.
- [x] **One accountant per campus — SHIPPED 2026-08-12 (F0).** See [[Finance Roles Plan]]. Raised by the operator: *"is there an accountant role, and is one accountant per campus the best option?"* **It was already per-campus and could not have been otherwise** — `UsersService` requires a campus for the role, and `restrictedCampusId()` returns `null` only for `OWNER_ADMIN` (everyone else falls back to `NO_CAMPUS`, which matches nothing). What was missing was a *count*: nothing stopped a campus having five. `ACCOUNTANT` now joins `CAMPUS_ADMIN` and `ADMISSION_CONTROLLER` as a campus seat.
  - **Both layers, because the code documents both.** `SOLE_CAMPUS_SEAT_ROLES` in the service **and** `users_one_accountant_per_campus` in `02_partial_uniques.sql` (partial on `deleted_at IS NULL`, so a departed accountant frees the seat at once). ⚠️ The plan had called this a one-line change and was wrong; the comment above the constant says the rule is enforced in the service *and* by an index per role.
  - **The 409 message is now label-driven.** It was a ternary over two roles that defaulted everything else to the campus-admin wording — a third seat would have told the operator their new accountant was "a campus admin".
  - ⚠️ **The probe distinguished the two layers rather than just confirming a failure.** Removing `ACCOUNTANT` from the seat list still refused the second accountant, but with **500** instead of 201: the index holds on its own. **The index is the safety under a race; the service check is what makes the refusal civil.**
  - ⚠️ **Costed before shipping:** only `OWNER_ADMIN` and `ACCOUNTANT` may take a payment, so a campus now has exactly one non-owner cashier — a single queue at the start of a fee month, with the owner as relief. Accepted deliberately; if it bites, the answer is a collect-only `CASHIER` seat, not a second accountant.
  - **F0b — the campuses screen now names the holder instead of offering the seat (2026-08-12).** Seat groups are singular and render whether filled or empty; a filled seat shows its holder, an empty one says what the campus cannot do. The add form disables a taken seat and names who holds it, and defaults to the first role that can actually be created (it used to default to `CAMPUS_ADMIN` — on a configured campus, the one choice guaranteed to 409). ⚠️ It immediately showed that **neither campus on the demo tenant has an accountant**, invisible before because an empty seat rendered as nothing. ⚠️ It also caught that **F0 had stale-dated a Playwright spec's premise and only jest had been re-run.** One new e2e case, probed in both halves; browser-verified.
  - **`FINANCE_VIEWER` (F1) was planned and dropped** on the operator's call — the school-wide financial view already reaches the owner on their dashboard. The all-or-nothing delegation gap behind it stays documented and unbuilt.
  - **Gates:** unit **62** · integration **901/901 across 36 suites** · isolation 7 · RLS coverage · lint · root typecheck · api+worker build.
- [x] **The owner's own login door — COMPLETE 2026-08-12 (O0–O3).** See [[Owner Login Plan]]. Raised by the operator: *"owner should have a separate login page — no other user should use that login page."* `POST /auth/owner-login` + `/owner-login`, owner-only, with a refusal **byte-identical to a wrong password** — a door that says *"you are not the owner"* is a detector that hands an attacker the one address worth attacking.
  - **The audit of the plan found more than the plan did.** Nine findings; **two were defects in the plan as written**. The critical one: `mfaChallenge()` issues a real session to anyone holding a valid `mfaToken`, and `login()` emits that token *before* issuing a session — so a door check placed after the MFA branch would have been a **complete bypass**, not a leak. The probe is two-step for exactly that reason: delete the check (door tests fail), then **move it below the MFA branch** — only the bypass test fails, and a weaker probe would have shown green while the hole shipped.
  - ⚠️ **A pre-existing defect surfaced: §22.3 account lockout has never fired.** The per-request `$transaction` rolls back `registerFailure()` when login throws, so `failedLoginCount` never rises. **Proven by a control test on the untouched `/auth/login` path** — the tempting read was "my new code is wrong". **Second inert security control in two days**, after the rate limiter; both were tested for their logic and never for their effect. Three tests marked `it.failing` assert the correct behaviour and flip to errors when it is fixed. **Not fixed here — bigger than the door, and a live brute-force exposure of its own.**
  - **One shared rate-limit bucket, deliberately.** The limiter keys on `rl:{policy}:{scope}:{id}` — the POLICY, not the route — so both doors share 5/IP/15min. A policy of its own would have given an attacker 5 per door and **halved** the protection the split was meant to improve.
  - **The MFA step was extracted, not copied** (`components/email-password-signin.tsx`): both doors return either a session or an `mfaToken` needing exchange, and a second copy would have been the `drainSms` mistake again.
  - ✅ **O2 + O3 SHIPPED the same day — the doors are now mutually exclusive.** `/login` refuses an `OWNER_ADMIN`, indistinguishably from a wrong password (the mirror-image oracle), and a dual-role owner is refused there too — a second role is not a way back in.
  - ⚠️ **34 of 36 integration suites went red the instant the staff door closed** — 868 of 910 tests — because every one signed in as the provisioned owner through its own hand-rolled `login()` wrapper. **Flipping the rule first and reading the failures beat grepping for call sites**; 33 files were then moved onto one shared `support/login.ts` helper by scripted transform, and its `auto` door (try staff, fall back to owner) kept the change to one line per file. The objection that a fallback masks a regression is answered by asserting the boundary in **exactly one place** rather than 34 incidental ones.
  - **The blast radius reached past the test suite:** `scripts/load-fees.mjs` defaults to `owner@demo.pk` and would have failed at sign-in on its next run; the Playwright helper's shared session moved to the owner door; and the dev prefill on `/login` was handing every developer the one credential that door now refuses (flagged in the O0 commit for exactly this moment, and moved to `/owner-login`).
  - ⚠️ **O3's own instruction was wrong and was reversed.** It called for permission-matrix rows on both doors — *"a route with no row is unmeasured"* — but that harness drives rows with a **session cookie** and asserts 403, while a login route is `@Public()`, takes credentials, and refuses with **401 deliberately indistinguishable from a wrong password**. A row would assert nothing while looking like coverage. Measured in `auth.e2e-spec.ts` instead, with a note in `permission-matrix.ts` where a reader would look for the missing rows.
  - ✅ **O4 — route naming, on operator request:** `/staff-login`, `/owner-login`, `/student-login`, with **`/login` kept as a neutral chooser**. ⚠️ Two of the proposed names were changed for cause: `/school-admin/login` sits one word from `/admin/login`, **the vendor console** (separate `PlatformUser` table, no RLS, its own cookies) — confusable in a runbook or firewall rule; and "management" excluded the teachers and office staff who are most of that door's traffic. ⚠️ **The bigger point was that `/login` cannot be a door at all**: it is the redirect target for every 401 and logout, where the visitor is signed out and nothing knows their role, so any single form there would refuse somebody on every session expiry. ⚠️ The rename also silently weakened three test guards (`!pathname.startsWith('/login')` is true immediately at `/staff-login`) — caught by reading, not by running.
  - **Gates:** unit 62 · integration **912/912 across 36 suites** · isolation 7 · Playwright **40** · lints · typechecks · api+worker+web builds.
- [x] **⚠️ §22.3 account lockout had NEVER fired — FIXED 2026-08-12.** Found while building the owner door, and pre-existing: `TenantTransactionInterceptor` wraps each request in one `$transaction`, and a failed login **throws** — so `registerFailure()`'s write was rolled back by the very exception it was counting. `failedLoginCount` never rose above 0, accounts never locked, and **nothing detected it because §22.3 had no test at all.**
  - **Proven pre-existing by a control test on the untouched `/auth/login` path**, because the tempting read was *"my new code is wrong"*. A wrong password left the counter at 0. **Second inert security control in two days** after the §29 rate limiter — both implemented, both reading correctly, neither ever running; both tested for their logic and never for their effect.
  - **Fixed with `TenantPrismaService.outsideRequestTransaction()`** — failure-path writes get a tenant transaction of their own. ⚠️ **A plain non-transactional client is not a substitute:** outside a transaction there is no `set_config('app.current_school_id')`, so RLS matches nothing and the write silently affects **zero rows** — failing closed and just as invisible as the rollback. `registerFailure()`, the lock self-heal and the wrong-door audit all use it; the self-heal **had** to move too, or the outer transaction's lock on that row would deadlock against the inner one.
  - **§22.3 finally has a test:** ten wrong passwords → `LOCKED` → an otherwise-correct password refused. Probed by putting the write back inside the request transaction — it fails along with the two door tests.
  - **Gates:** unit 62 · integration **910/910 across 36 suites** · isolation 7 · Playwright 40 · lints · typechecks · builds.
- [x] **Fees billing — B0 + B1 SHIPPED 2026-08-12.** See [[Fees Billing Plan]]. Raised by the operator working the fee screens: *"when the fee controller collects, he should just select the student and hit generate."*
  - **B0 — the invoice key is now enforced for every origin.** The unique index was `WHERE batch_id IS NOT NULL`, constraining only batch-generated rows; the per-student path creates invoices with **no batch** and would have landed in the uncovered half — a child billable twice for one month with no complaint from the database. ⚠️ **The plan's own first draft would have shipped that hole**, caught by auditing the plan before building it. Now `fee_invoices_one_per_student_month`, predicated on `month IS NOT NULL` — explicit, because NULLs are DISTINCT and an unconditional index would imply cover it cannot give.
  - **B1 — `POST /fees/invoices` bills one student.** Closes a real hole: a (class, month, year) can be billed **once ever**, so a child admitted after their class's batch ran could not be invoiced at all, and mid-session admissions are normal. Idempotent like `pay`, audited as `INVOICE_GENERATED`, campus-checked off the student's own ACTIVE enrolment.
  - **One pricing path, not two:** `buildInvoice()` is shared by the batch and the single-student route. Three rules must agree — effective-dated prices, the ANNUAL anchor month, discounts — and billing two different ways depending on the button pressed is worse than billing wrongly once.
  - ⚠️ **The authz was widened and caught on review**: the first draft admitted `CAMPUS_ADMIN`, wider than the batch route it parallels. Invariant J2 failing on its own author within the hour.
  - ⚠️ **A test failed for a fixture reason that read as a product bug** — a reused guardian phone made `admit` return no `studentId`, surfacing as *"studentId must be a UUID"*. The test now asserts the admission first.
  - **Gates:** unit 62 · integration **920/920 across 36 suites** · isolation 7 · RLS coverage · lints · typechecks · api+worker builds. Probed in both halves.
  - ✅ **B2 + B3 SHIPPED 2026-08-13, completing the plan.**
  - **B2 — the sibling discount now applies.** `siblingDiscountPercent` had lived in the schema, the DTO, the API types **and the settings screen** while being read by nothing: an owner could set 20%, see a success toast, and no invoice was ever a rupee cheaper. Siblings come from the shared **primary guardian** (`StudentGuardian.isPrimary`, which the CSV import already uses to link families by phone), ranked by admission order among the **currently enrolled**. The reason rides on the invoice — *"Sibling discount (child 2, 20%)"* — which is what makes historising the setting unnecessary.
  - **B3 — the admission fee can be billed at all.** Billing asked *"is it this month?"*, which no one-off charge can answer, so `ADMISSION`/`ONE_TIME` were silently skipped and one of the largest charges in a Pakistani school was collected off-book. Now a ledger fact: charged once **per enrolment**, so a promotion does not re-charge it.
  - ✅ **A guard was doing its job right up to the end.** `fee-setup.service` refused those frequencies outright — *"Invoicing does not yet charge ADMISSION fees, so this would never appear on a bill"* — which is why the gap was discoverable rather than silent. B3 removed the reason; the guard went with it and the comment stayed, because the pattern is worth copying.
  - ⚠️ **An existing test asserted the opposite and was rewritten, not deleted** — a test that encodes a decision is evidence. ⚠️ **The first sibling fixture built the wrong family** (two `CREATE` guardians with one phone are two parents, not siblings), which read as "the feature does not work".
  - ⚠️ **Two attendance failures in the final run were pre-existing, proven by stashing every change and re-running** — the same two fail on a clean tree. ✅ **Fixed straight after (see the timezone entry below): both were TEST bugs, and the product was right.**
  - **Gates:** unit 62 · integration **923/925** (the 2 pre-existing attendance flakes above) · isolation 7 · lints · typechecks · api+worker+web builds. Probed separately for B2 and B3 — each breaks only its own cases.
- [x] **Attendance timezone tests corrected — 2026-08-13.** Two cases failed every evening after 19:00 UTC. ⚠️ **Both were the TESTS' fault; `closureNotice()` and `isPastLocalTime()` resolve the school's own clock correctly** — established by reading them and by stashing all local changes to confirm the failures on a clean tree. I had told the operator this was "a real latent bug in the closure handling"; **it was not, and the correction is the point** — the product had the harder half right.
  - **An offset is not an ordering.** *"Karachi is +5, so its clock reads later"* is false for five hours a day: past 19:00 UTC it reads 00:xx, **earlier** than UTC's 19:xx, so a deadline placed just past the UTC clock inverted the expectation. The case now sorts the two zones by actual `HH:MM` and puts the deadline between them, whichever way round they sit.
  - **A test asserting timezone correctness must not compute its own dates in UTC.** The closure case created a holiday for the SERVER's date, so after 19:00 UTC it was yesterday's to the tenant and the endpoint correctly returned `closure: null`. It now reads the timezone from the tenant's settings rather than hard-coding it.
  - **Green at one hour is not green:** proved general by swapping the zone pair for one reading *later* than UTC right now (`Europe/Moscow`) — both orderings pass at the same instant.
  - **Gates:** unit 62 · integration **925/925 across 36 suites** · isolation 7 · lints · typechecks. First fully green run of the day.
- [x] **UI retheme U0–U5 SHIPPED 2026-08-15.** See [[UI Retheme Plan]] and [[School-Admin.pk Design Reference]]. Applied the reference's design *language* to `apps/web` — tokens, a two-font split, a navy card-header pattern, pill CTAs, brand-tinted shadows.
  - **A retheme, not a rewrite — decided by evidence.** `apps/web` has no Tailwind: one hand-rolled `globals.css` driving a semantic class vocabulary that **71 Playwright assertions depend on**. Migrating to utilities would have broken the suite that gates every other change, in exchange for nothing the variables could not already do. The 15-class vocabulary is unchanged; 5 classes were added alongside it.
  - **The identity was deliberately shifted off the reference.** School-Admin.pk is a direct competitor with 500+ schools; copying their exact palette and font pairing would have made us look like the incumbent. Patterns taken, hues moved: brand `#17365c` (not their `#234777`), accent `#a1741c` (not their `#df9118`), **Bitter** headings (not Roboto Slab). Both fonts self-hosted via `next/font` — the CSP would block a CDN.
  - ⚠️ **The retheme broke contrast that had been passing by 0.01, everywhere at once.** `--muted` `#6b7280` measured **4.51** on the old `--bg` `#f6f7f9`. Moving `--bg` to the blue-grey `#eef2f8` dropped the same pair to **4.30**, putting every muted-on-field label under the floor on every screen. Now `#5b6472` (5.32 on `--bg`, 5.98 on `--card`) — chosen with headroom precisely because the old value proved that sitting on the threshold is not passing.
  - ⚠️ **The sidebar group labels had never been legible:** slate-500 `#64748b` on the navy sidebar is **3.28**. On a dark fill the ramp inverts — pick the light end, not the muted end. Now `#94a3b8` at 6.08.
  - ⚠️ **`app/manifest.ts` was left behind by the retheme**, and it is the one surface where that is most visible: the installed app painted the **old** brand on the Android status bar and the **old** field on the splash. The manifest is generated in TS and cannot read a CSS custom property, so `--brand` lives in three places that must move together — `globals.css`, `layout.tsx` `themeColor`, `manifest.ts` `theme_color`. `installable.spec.ts` now asserts all of it, including the manifest colours, which nothing covered before.
  - **Contrast was audited by computing rendered pairs off the live DOM**, compositing alpha layers up to the first opaque background — not by eyeballing tokens. Eight failures on the dashboard, then zero across dashboard, students, fees, classes, the workbench, campuses and all four login doors, at 375/768/1440. Every hard-coded hex left in the stylesheet was measured too (`.marks` 4.83–6.47, `.now` 6.71/10.35, sidebar 10.50/12.22). **K2 holds: `--accent` is never a text colour** — its one text use is `--accent-ink` `#7a570f` at 6.57.
  - **K3 teacher shell green:** four tabs at 375, sidebar at 1440, `/home` reachable, `group-label` count 0 for teachers.
  - **Gates:** unit 62 · integration 925 · isolation 7 · **Playwright 37 passed / 5 skipped / 0 failed** · lints · typechecks · api+worker+web builds.
- [x] **Two more attendance tests were wrong about the school, not the product — 2026-08-15.** Both surfaced during the U5 run because **it was a Saturday**, and both were proven pre-existing by stashing every UI change and re-running.
  - **`staff-attendance` skipped nothing every Saturday.** Its guard matched `/holiday or weekly off/i` — copy the app had *deliberately* moved away from, so a teacher could tell a shut school from a broken button. It matched neither branch, fell through, and failed on the correctly-absent Check-in button. Re-anchored on *"no attendance is taken"*, the sentence both branches share. ⚠️ **It also had a race:** `isVisible()` does not auto-wait and the control renders from its own fetch, so the guard read a page where *neither* outcome existed yet. Now waits on `button.or(nonWorking)` first — same defect and same fix as the `fee-claims` loading race.
  - **`safeAttendanceDate()` asserted a configuration this tenant never had.** It walked back over Sundays only, commented *"the school's default weekly-off (SUNDAY)"* — but the demo tenant keeps **Saturday and Sunday**. It now reads the school's own `weeklyOffDays`. ⚠️ **And closures, which a first pass dismissed as a remote edge case:** the very next run walked back off Saturday onto **14 August, Independence Day**. "Rare" and "every year on a fixed date" are not the same thing.
  - ⚠️ **The deeper constraint, now documented in the spec:** `StudentEnrollment.startedAt` is `@default(now())` with no API override, and the register refuses any date the enrolment was not active on — deliberately, so backfilling cannot invent a record for a child who had not joined. **So a freshly seeded student can only ever be marked TODAY**, and on a weekly off there is no legal date at all. The spec now skips saying exactly that, rather than reaching for `allowHolidayOverride`, which would silently convert it into a test of a different claim.
  - **Both fixes were proven non-vacuous by making today a working day** (weekly off temporarily `["SUNDAY"]`, restored after): each test body passes fully, rather than being green because it skips.
- [x] **V0–V2 — a real icon set, and a dashboard that can actually be read — 2026-08-15.** Raised by the operator against the retheme: *"the icons are not colour scheme specific, they are just random icons"*, and *"in the reference there were charts … in this dashboard there is nothing like this"*. Both were correct, and both had a root cause the retheme could not have fixed by itself.
  - ⚠️ **`apps/web` contained ZERO SVG — every icon in the product was an emoji**, 23 of them in `NAV` alone. An emoji is a text glyph the OS paints in its own fixed multi-colour way: it **cannot inherit `currentColor`**, so it cannot be brand-tinted, cannot go white on a navy header, cannot dim when disabled, and is a different picture on Windows, macOS, Android and iOS. That is precisely why they read as "random" against a deliberate palette — **they were never part of it and could not be.** No amount of token work would have changed that.
  - **`components/icon.tsx`**: one 24×24 stroke set on `currentColor`, 1.75px, round caps, sharing a single `<svg>` wrapper so box and colour behaviour cannot drift per icon. `NavItem.icon` was retyped from `string` to `IconName`, so a future nav entry **cannot** reintroduce an emoji — and the compiler immediately found four more the eye had missed (the staff-register statuses).
  - ⚠️ **`GET /dashboard` returned eight scalars and no series, so no chart was possible at all.** "Add charts" was never a frontend task. The fix was nearly free: `DashboardService` already fetched today's records **with `status`**, computed a percentage and **threw the breakdown away**. Counting them adds no query. `collectionsTrend` buckets six months from one ranged read — not six queries, and not raw SQL, which would bypass the tenant extension.
  - ⚠️ **The obvious chart palette is unreadable, and it is the one the reference site uses.** Measured, not judged: absent `#e40014` ↔ present `#00a544` is **ΔE 5.3 under deuteranopia** — below even the conditional floor — and present-vs-absent is the most important distinction on a school register. late `#f05100` ↔ absent is **ΔE 8.8 under NORMAL vision**, below the floor of 15. Fixed by **ordering the bands so red and green never touch** (blue `leave` sits between them — segment order is a real accessibility control, since only neighbours touch) and stepping `late` to `#c2410c`: worst adjacent pair now 7.2 deutan / 30.0 normal, all bands ≥3:1 on white. 7.2 is legal **only** with secondary encoding, so the 2px gaps and the labelled legend are load-bearing rather than decorative.
  - ⚠️ **The charts were built in SVG first, and that was wrong.** They looked right on a laptop and failed on a phone: **the peak label measured 7px tall at 375px**, because a `viewBox` scales its own text down with the width. Rebuilt as plain HTML boxes — a stacked bar is a row of widths, a column chart a row of heights — where type is real CSS px at every width. Two clipping bugs (label bbox at y−8.2, and a `min-height` that drew **true zeros as 2px stubs**) were found by measuring in the browser, not by looking.
  - **What the register chart now shows is the point:** the dashboard reads *"Today's attendance 100%"* while the bar shows **94% not marked**. `unmarked` is plotted as a band rather than omitted, because a part-to-whole chart *claims* its parts sum to the whole — leaving the remainder out would make the picture structurally false.
  - **Gates:** unit 62 · integration 925 · isolation 7 · lint (api + web) · typechecks · api+worker+web builds · live browser verification at 375/1440 with a zero-failure contrast sweep. ⚠️ **Playwright could NOT be run** — see the environment note below.
- [x] **V3 — the stat tile's hover and overflow — 2026-08-17.** Reported from screenshots: *"as i hover over the container it does not look good and is hiding the text behind"*. Two independent defects that happened to surface together.
  - ⚠️ **`.stat` and `.metric-link` had opposite intents and were composed on one element.** `.stat`'s own comment defines it as a bare ROW; `.metric-link` bolts CARD behaviour on — white fill, hover lift, 32px shadow. Measured: `padding: 0`, `border: 0 none`, `border-radius: 0`. So hover drew a hard-edged white slab tight around the text with a shadow hanging off it, on an element that had none of a card's box. Worse, `.metric-link:hover` sets `border-color` only, and **a colour on a zero-width border paints nothing** — the intended outline was dead code, leaving the shadow as the whole affordance.
  - **Fixed by scoping the lift to what is actually a card.** `.metric.metric-link:hover` keeps the lift (staff-attendance's tiles are real bordered cards and were designed for it); `.stat.metric-link:hover` tints and outlines **in place** — no transform, so no stacking context, so it can never paint over a neighbour. `.stat` also gained real padding, radius and a **transparent 1px border** so the hover colour has something to land on and hover cannot shift layout by a pixel.
  - ⚠️ **The text-hiding was a separate, structural bug: the tile laid out icon + numeral + caption as three flex children in a ROW**, so its width was their *sum*. "Rs 90,000.00 / Collections (month)" needed **311px in a 228px cell** and spilled **73px into the next tile**; `transform` on hover then opened a stacking context and the neighbour's opaque white background covered the spilled caption — confirmed with `elementFromPoint`. Stacking the numeral over its caption makes the width `max(numeral, caption)` instead of the sum, which is what the design reference does. Grid minimum also raised 200 → 220px.
  - ⚠️ **Shrinking the numeral silently changed which contrast floor applied — the second time in this stylesheet.** Money dropped to 18.27px, just under WCAG's 18.66px bold "large text" line, so its floor jumped 3:1 → 4.5:1 and `--ok` at **3.25** began failing. The rule's own comment had predicted it exactly (*"--ok and --warn PASS HERE AND NOWHERE SMALLER"*), so `.stat--money` now takes the `-ink` variants (6.3–9.2 on both white and the hover tint). **A font-size change is a contrast change.**
  - **Verified:** no tile overflows and none spills at 375 / 768 / 1280 (the money tile went from +87px over to 34px of slack); real hover driven with a mouse gives tint + brand border, `transform: none`, `box-shadow: none`, **0 sibling overlaps**; zero contrast failures at all three widths; `.metric` cards on staff-attendance keep box and lift; lint + typecheck + web build green.
  - ⚠️ **Two readings were measurement artifacts, not defects** — both the hover border-colour and (earlier) the focus ring read as transparent because the Browser pane was not compositing, which pins a *transitioned* property at its start value. `border-color` is in `.metric-link`'s transition list and `background` is not, which is exactly why one appeared to apply and the other did not. Disable the transition before believing a computed value in this harness.
- [x] ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-08-17 — dev environment: `*.localhost` had stopped resolving on this machine.** `dns.lookup('demo.localhost')` returns `ENOTFOUND` (0/10 attempts), and `Test-NetConnection` agrees, with no hosts entry present. **Every** request through the Next dev proxy dies on it, because both the tenant rewrite **and** `FALLBACK_ORIGIN` in `apps/web/next.config.mjs` target `http://demo.localhost:4000` — so even Playwright's bare `localhost:3001` baseURL routes through that name. The browser suite therefore cannot run.
  - **Not a product defect and not caused by the UI work:** `next.config.mjs` and `playwright.config.ts` are untouched, and the API answers **200** on `http://127.0.0.1:4000` when the Host header carries the tenant — so the API, the tenant resolution and the proxy shape are all correct. The only broken link is name → IP.
  - **Chromium resolves `*.localhost` itself (RFC 6761), which is why every live browser check still passed** — and is why this can hide until something server-side needs the same name.
  - **Fixed** by adding `127.0.0.1 demo.localhost` to `C:\Windows\System32\drivers\etc\hosts`, with the operator's approval. `node dns.lookup` now returns `127.0.0.1` and the proxied login returns 200.
