  - ⚠️ **`Add-Content` could not do it — it threw `ArgumentException: Stream was not readable`,** and an earlier run left a comment line behind while the mapping line silently vanished. That is not an access-denied: the file is held open by real-time scanning. **Read-modify-write in ONE atomic `[System.IO.File]::WriteAllText` succeeded** where streaming an append did not. Worth remembering for any future system-file edit on this machine.
  - Also note `Start-Process -Verb RunAs` cannot be combined with `-RedirectStandardOutput` (different parameter sets), so an elevated helper has to report back through a file.
  - **With it resolved, the full Playwright suite ran green for the first time in four commits: 40 passed / 2 skipped / 0 failed.**
- [x] **Timetable & bell schedule — COMPLETE 2026-08-17 (P0–P5), plan audited before building.** See [[Timetable & Bell Schedule Plan]]. Raised by the operator: *"how are periods assigned to a class, their duration, the number of periods?"* **The placement half has been built since TT0 and is correct; the shape half does not exist.** `periodNo` is a bare ordinal — no start time, no end time, no duration anywhere in the schema, `SchoolSettings` or the DTO — and the period count is not declared but **guessed**: `gridShape()` reads `max(periodNo)` over rows that happen to exist, floored at 6. So an 8-period school sees 6 rows until somebody types into period 7, and there is no answer to *"how many periods does Grade 9 have?"* beyond *"how many have been typed so far"*.
  - ⚠️ **The grid is rectangular and this market is not.** Friday is a short day in virtually every Pakistani school, and its empty cells cannot distinguish *"not filled yet"* from *"Friday has no period 6"* — the same distinction `coverage()` exists one level up to preserve.
  - **Also missing:** breaks/lunch/assembly cannot be represented at all; subject load (*"Maths 6 a week"*) is not modelled, so nothing can say a grid is two periods short; and the only write is one cell per request — **720 saves** for a 15-section school at 8×6, with no copy-day and no template.
  - **Four decisions taken:** bell schedule per campus with a wing override · subject load **advisory**, mirroring `sectionCapacityMode` · the admin composes **each day completely** (periods, breaks, durations, same or different per day — which is why `dayOfWeek` sits on `BellPeriod` and nothing presumes a shape) · cell-by-cell plus copy tools, keeping the existing editor's five rules untouched.
  - ⚠️ **Copy-day and copy-section are not the same problem**, and shipping them together would ship one broken: two sections of one class running the same grid clash on the same teacher at **every** row, and `staffId` is NOT NULL so there is no "copy subjects, leave teachers blank". P3 ships copy-day; copy-section is deferred with its own decision, recorded rather than quietly dropped.
  - **The timing runs the safe way for once:** `timetable_slots` holds no production data in any school, so every new constraint lands on an empty table. The same work after schools build their grids means reconciling real data against a schedule invented afterwards. **Highest-value thing deliberately NOT in the plan: copy-year** — slots are year-scoped, so today a rollover rebuilds every grid by hand.
  - ⚠️ **The plan was audited before any code was written and FOUR SERIOUS DEFECTS were found in the plan itself** (12 findings total; full table in §13 of the plan). **A1 was critical:** the draft modelled the wing override as `classes Class[]`, an *implicit* Prisma many-to-many — which creates a join table with **no `school_id`**, therefore no RLS policy, therefore invisible to all three isolation layers at once. Verified while auditing: **0** implicit m2m tables exist across 37 migrations; every m2m in this schema is an explicit tenant-chained model. Fixed with an explicit `BellScheduleClass`.
  - ⚠️ **A2 — I created the only real ambiguity in the model myself.** The operator's four decisions never mentioned seasonal schedules; the draft added `effectiveFrom/To` unasked, and that alone let one section resolve to two schedules on different dates while `TimetableSlot.periodNo` could not say which it was authored against. **The wing override never had the problem** (a section sits in one class, one schedule). Seasonal variant **cut from v1**; Ramadan is handled by editing the timings, with a retain-and-warn rule so lessons in dropped periods are kept and reappear.
  - ⚠️ **A3 — the draft contradicted itself in a way that would have shipped a breaking change:** it required `setSlot` to validate against the schedule *and* promised that a school with no schedule keeps a working editor. No schedule ⇒ no teaching rows ⇒ every write fails. The check is now conditional.
  - **A4 turned a validation rule into a structural impossibility.** Row-level editing cannot uphold a whole-day contiguity invariant, so the day is written atomically and **the client sends durations while the server computes the times** — a gap or overlap is now inexpressible rather than rejected.
  - **The lesson:** the most valuable finding was not about timetables at all — see the RLS-gate entry below. **Auditing the plan was cheaper than auditing the product, and it found a product defect.**
  - ✅ **BUILT the same day, all six phases.** `bell_schedules`/`bell_schedule_classes`/`bell_periods`, a whole-day `PUT`, the *School Timings* screen, the grid rendering the declared day, advisory subject load, copy-a-day, and the wing override. **Gates: unit 69 · integration 991/991 across 37 suites · isolation 7 · Playwright 42 passed / 2 skipped / 0 failed · matrix 562 · RLS coverage + the new enrolment check · migration safety · lint (api+web) · root/e2e/web typechecks · api+worker builds.**
  - ⚠️ **The design that paid off most: the client sends DURATIONS, never times.** The server walks the day from its start, so a gap or an overlap is not rejected — there is no field in which to express one. Same shape as `Student.userId` making cross-student portal access structurally impossible rather than guarded.
  - ⚠️ **Probed nine ways, and one probe was worth all the others.** Making the period check *unconditional* fails **14 tests, 13 of them in the pre-existing timetable spec** — which is precisely what every school with a grid and no timings would have hit on deploy. The audit's A3 caught that as a contradiction on paper; the probe is what proved the size of it.
  - **Two things the plan asked for were deliberately NOT done, both recorded rather than dropped quietly:** breaks are not drawn as bands in the week grid (each day is composed independently, so one band across seven columns draws a break on days that do not have it), and `gridShape()` was kept as the fallback rather than deleted (deleting it contradicts the conditional check — a school with no timings still needs a grid).
  - ⚠️ **`§3.1`'s own comment named the nested-create trap and the first draft of `create()` walked into it anyway.** Writing a rule down is not the same as following it; the tests caught it as four 500s.
  - ⚠️ **A test-isolation hole found while writing the load tests:** `periodsPerWeek` and `section_subjects` are both sticky and both feed the meter, so without an `afterEach` reset the "unallocated is not zero" case passed or failed on **test order** rather than on behaviour.
  - **Verified live on demo:** Mon–Thu 4 periods (08:15–08:55 after a 15-minute assembly), **Friday 2** (08:00–08:40), Friday periods 3–4 rendering **—**, no Sat/Sun column, and a second wing over an already-claimed class refused with *"8th already follows \"Primary Wing\". A class can only follow one schedule."* Demo left with the `Regular` default in place (the tenant holds zero timetable slots, so nothing is affected) and the test wing retired.
  - **The timetable got its FIRST Playwright spec** — it had 19 integration cases and no browser coverage at all, the only major screen without any.
- [x] ⚠️ **The RLS coverage gate had a structural blind spot — FOUND AND FIXED 2026-08-17, shipped in the timetable plan's P0.** `scripts/check-rls-coverage.mjs` selects `FROM information_schema.columns WHERE column_name = 'school_id'` and reports any such table lacking `tenant_isolation`. **A tenant table WITHOUT a `school_id` column is therefore never a candidate and passes green** — the gate can only police tables that already opted in. `05_rls.sql` loops on the same condition, and the Prisma extension keys on `schoolId` too, so all three layers share one assumption: *a tenant table has a `school_id` column*.
  - **Currently theoretical — there are no such tables** — which is exactly why it reads as stronger than it is. It became concrete the moment a plan reached for `classes Class[]` (above): Prisma's implicit join table has only `A`/`B` columns, would have carried tenant data, and would have shipped through a green gate.
  - ✅ **Fixed and proven in both directions.** A complementary assertion now fails on any `public` table outside the allowlist (`schools`, `platform_users`, `platform_refresh_tokens`, `_prisma_migrations`) that lacks `school_id`. **Proven non-vacuous by creating a bare `_BellScheduleToClass` table — exactly what Prisma's implicit m2m produces — and watching `✔ RLS coverage` print while it sat there unprotected, then watching the new check fail with exit 1.** A gate that checks nothing passes loudly, which this repo learned once already from the `tsconfig` exclude.
- [x] ⚠️ **A malformed `:id` answered 500, on almost every route — FIXED 2026-08-17.** Filed during Cover C4 and open since. `ParseUUIDPipe` was on **4 of 85** path parameters; everywhere else the raw string reached Prisma and came back as `Inconsistent column data: Error creating UUID` — a 500, an error-rate alert and a stack trace, for what is a caller's typo.
  - **Not cosmetic.** It had already cost real debugging time by making a **bad fixture read as a broken feature**: `POST /exams/undefined/...` returned 500, which says *investigate the server*, and the server was fine.
  - ⚠️ **Fixed TWICE, and the second version replaced the first.** The first pass put `ParseUUIDPipe` on **81 call sites across 18 controllers** — it worked, shipped, and was **the wrong shape**: a per-route pipe has to be remembered again for every route added afterwards, so it decays silently. Replaced the same day by **one `UuidParamPipe` bound as `APP_PIPE`**, which covers every id-carrying route by construction, including ones written later.
  - **The better design came from `claude/busy-kare-b900d0`** — an earlier session's fix, sitting unmerged and 10k lines behind `main`. It was nearly deleted as stale debris. **Reading a branch before deleting it turned out to be worth more than the branch.**
  - **Name-based, not blanket:** the pipe matches `id` and any `<entity>Id`, leaving `:token` (the public guardian fee link) and `:provider` (the SMS webhook) alone. `:gid` was renamed `:guardianId` so the naming convention IS the rule and the pipe needs no special case.
  - ⚠️ **Version-agnostic, deliberately — the subtlety the first pass missed entirely.** `test/matrix` drives most of its rows against the **nil UUID** as a deliberately-absent id, and the nil UUID is not v4. A v4-strict pipe would collapse *well-formed but not found* (404) into *malformed* (400) — the exact distinction the pipe exists to draw. Format is the edge's business; existence is the service's.
  - ⚠️ **Bound in `AppModule`, not `main.ts`.** Every integration spec builds its app from `AppModule` and re-declares only the `ValidationPipe`, so a pipe registered in the bootstrap file would be **present in production and absent from all 38 suites**.
  - **The e2e enumerates routes from the live Express router** rather than listing them, with a floor assertion (>50 found) so a broken enumeration fails loudly instead of sweeping nothing. The hand-listed version it replaced named three routes that **do not exist** — they returned 404 because nothing matched, which would have left those cases permanently green.
  - **Proven non-vacuous across the whole surface: with the pipe unbound the sweep names 61 routes returning 500; with it, zero.** (61 rather than the original branch's 60 — the bell-schedule routes are now covered too.)
  - **Gates:** unit 87 · integration 997/997 across 38 suites · isolation 7 · lint · typecheck · api+worker builds. Fewer test *cases* than the 1002 of the hand-listed version, and far more routes actually covered.
- [ ] **Information architecture / categorisation — PLANNED 2026-08-18, decisions open, nothing built.** See [[Information Architecture Plan]]. Raised by the operator from two screenshots: the Classes page and the Staff page **both report 24 subjects with no teacher**, and neither is where you fix it.
  - ⚠️ **The headline finding is a correctness bug, not a layout one: that number is computed TWICE by two different mechanisms.** `/classes` joins sections × subjects × assignments **in the browser**; `/staff` reads a **server-computed** `coverageGaps`. They agree today and nothing makes them agree tomorrow — the same shape as the three attendance percentages that once gave a parent, a teacher and a director three different figures for one child. **S0 deletes the client-side copy and ships first even if nothing else does.**
  - **Campus is a filter where it should be a level.** The code already partitions by campus (`groups`), but renders it as a flat heading in one long scroll — and **Campus Hub sits in Administration, three nav groups away from the classes it contains.**
  - **Subjects have no home at all.** `GET /subjects/catalogue` exists and **has never had a caller**, so "which classes teach Chemistry, and who teaches it where" cannot be asked — and `periodsPerWeek`, added by the timetable work, has one editing surface buried inside a single class.
  - **Academics carries 9 of 22 nav items**, mixing what the school IS (yearly), what happens today (hourly), and what it produced (read-only). Proposed: seven groups, none over five, with **Campuses moved beside what it contains**.
  - ⚠️ **The nav regroup is the riskiest phase for the least visible gain** — `navItemFor` gates on `href`, so moving an item between groups is safe but **renaming one would silently open the route to everyone**. Items move, hrefs do not, and the matrix is re-run.
  - ⚠️ **AUDITED THE SAME DAY AND FAILED ON ITS CENTRAL CLAIM: it was not a whole-system plan.** It generalised from two screens and proposed fixes shaped like those two screens. Counted from the code, the same patterns are on **12 screens, 31 tiles and 18 endpoints**. §8 of the plan is the audit, §9 is the plan it should have been.
  - ⚠️ **The biggest UX defect in the product, missed by v1 entirely: 31 metric tiles, ZERO clickable.** Every headline number — "24 Without a teacher", "13 Subjects taught", "17 of 720 seats" — is a dead end. Highest leverage and lowest risk of anything in the plan: no data, permission or layout change, and it turns every existing summary into an entry point.
  - ⚠️ **18 API capabilities ship with NO user interface**, found by walking all 248 controller routes. Includes **no password change and no forgot-password** (an owner resets staff passwords by hand), **no SMS admin at all** in a product that texts parents fee reminders and absences, **no fee waive or payment reversal UI** despite both being audited financial actions, and no payroll editing surface. **No gate notices this** — a route can ship, pass its tests, carry a matrix row and be unreachable by a human.
  - **Campus is a filter on 12 screens**, each with its own selector and no shared context — so a campus admin sees a chooser for a choice they cannot make, twelve times, and "switch campus" is not an action the product has. v1 proposed fixing it on one screen.
  - **A second duplicated derived fact** beyond the gaps: `/classes` aggregates seats client-side while `/reports/class-strength` answers the same question server-side.
  - **Reordered by leverage ÷ risk:** clickable tiles → one source per fact → shared campus context → the missing screens (password reset first) → Classes/Subjects → **nav regroup LAST**, because v1's own risk section called it the riskiest phase for the least gain while scheduling it second.
  - **What is NOT broken, stated because an audit that only finds faults is not measuring:** nav↔route mapping is clean in both directions — no orphaned routes, no dangling links.
  - **The target shape is now written: [[System Structure]]** — five spines, the containment tree, **five laws (one per audit finding)**, the navigation with all 18 missing capabilities placed, and countable success criteria the plan lacked. *The IA Plan is the analysis; System Structure is the design.*
  - ⚠️ **Correction that strengthens the tile finding:** "0 clickable" is right for the 31 `.metric` tiles, but the product **already has the pattern** — `.metric-link` is defined in `globals.css` with hover, focus-visible and a documented `.metric.metric-link` variant, and is already used on `/dashboard` and `/staff-attendance`. It is not a design gap; it is **an existing, styled, accessible component that 31 tiles on ten screens do not use.**
  - **Two of the eighteen gaps are not new screens, and placing them right matters more than adding nav items:** **change password belongs on `/security`**, which already exists and already handles MFA — a field, not a screen — and **forgot-password is pre-login**, so it belongs on the login doors and in no navigation at all.
  - **Success criteria, countable before and after:** dead tiles 31→0 · screens with their own campus selector 12→0 · routes with no UI and no listed exception 18→0 · derived facts with >1 implementation 2→0 · largest nav group 9→≤5.
  - ✅ **IA0 shipped** (581e242): 31 tiles now link/filter/context. ✅ **IA1 shipped**: the teacher-gap fact has ONE server implementation (`SetupService.coverageGaps`); `/classes` reads it instead of re-deriving, `/staff` delegates to it, and a spec asserts both endpoints return the same set (probed: removing scoping fails it). ⚠️ **Correction found while building: the "seats filled" duplication was NOT real** — `section.enrolled` is server-computed and the client only sums it, so IA1 left it alone. Verifying beat acting. ✅ **IA2 shipped** (option B, the *campus lens*): an owner with >1 campus sets the branch once in the shell and every oversight screen follows — staff/staff-attendance/performance/students dropped their own campus selects, classes grouping collapses to the lensed branch, and add-class/timings/admissions pre-fill it. ⚠️ **Scope shrank honestly** — the audit's "12 selectors" was ~2 redundant; the shipped value is the multi-campus director's daily branch-switch (the Boys/Girls-campus case). Verified live (switch → narrow → persist → reload-restore) and Playwright 42/2-skip/0-fail. `setup` deliberately NOT lensed (it manages campuses). ⚠️ Also learned the hard way: running `next build` in the live `next dev` dir corrupted `.next` and 500'd the server — recorded in Key Decisions. ✅ **IA3 shipped**: the **route-coverage gate** (`route-coverage.e2e-spec.ts`) — reads the LIVE Express router and fails on any route with no `apps/web` caller and no listed exception. ⚠️ The audit's "18 routes" was wrong three ways (per-file prefix bug; substring heuristic); the gate found **19 genuine gaps** and correctly EXCLUDED the 7 `/reports/*` (reached via dynamic `apiGet(`/reports/${key}`)`). First missing screen built: **SMS admin** (`/sms`) — templates/credits/logs/retry — closing a real blind spot (the product texts parents with zero visibility). ⚠️ Building it exposed a **latent over-exposure**: the SMS read routes had NO `@Roles` and were readable by any staff (logs carry parent phone numbers); now admin-only. Backlog (in the gate, un-loseable): finance admin (waive/reversals/advances/defaulters/integrity-check), payroll, student-record actions (guardians/withdraw/report-cards/OTP), documents, audit-logs, promotions, student-leave cancel, /sms/send, change-password (deferred). Gates: route-coverage green, matrix 598 (incl. 5 new SMS rows), lints, typechecks, live-verified. ✅ **IA4 shipped**: the **Subjects screen** (`/subjects`) — the cross-class view that never existed. `GET /subjects/catalogue` had a bare `{name, classCount}` and NO caller; enriched to fold subjects by name with their classes, per-class `periodsPerWeek` (editable inline, same `setLoad` as the class page), and teacher-gap counts that **reuse `coverageGaps()`** (Law 4) — so "Without a teacher: 24" here equals /classes and /staff by construction. The /classes "Subjects taught" tile now links here. ⚠️ Same latent-authz pattern as SMS: `GET /subjects/catalogue` had no `@Roles` (open to any staff) and now carries gap oversight data — gated to admins, matrix row added. Verified live (13 subjects, gap total 24 matching, periods/week edit round-trips). Gates: integration 1040/1040 across 39 suites, lints, typechecks. ✅ **IA5 shipped — the IA arc is COMPLETE.** Nav regrouped into seven spine-based groups, none over five: Academics split into **School structure** (Classes, Subjects, Timetable, School Timings, Campus Hub) and **Teaching** (Attendance, Leave requests, Cover, Exams); Reports + Performance moved to **Overview**; **Campus Hub** moved out of Administration. ⚠️ The plan's whole risk was a silent href/role change (navItemFor gates on href); **proven safe three ways** — only `group:`/type/order lines changed, the href set is byte-identical, and the (href→roles) mapping is byte-identical. Verified live: the owner's sidebar renders exactly the seven groups in order. Gates: matrix 603 + route-coverage green, Playwright 40 passed / 2 skipped. ⚠️ **Two Playwright reds were NOT IA5**: classes-ux was a cold-compile flake (passed on retry); the fees `generate invoice batch` spec fails on a pre-existing demo-tenant data-state ("no unbilled month found in the next two years" — the tenant is billed for every month) — **proven pre-existing by failing identically on `main`**. Worth its own fix: the fees e2e should provision a fresh year rather than depend on an unbilled month in the shared demo tenant. ✅ **FIXED 2026-08-19**: the real fix could NOT be tenant isolation (admitting needs an ADMISSION_CONTROLLER seat the demo pre-seeds, and provisioning a fresh tenant would have to recreate it) nor deletion (the teardown deliberately preserves payment-bearing students — financial records are not swept). The reused fee class exhausts months because a `(class, month, year)` batch is billed once and never deleted. Fix: **jump past the billing frontier** — page through invoices for the true max billed month and bill the next slot, one read-set + one generate. Never exhausts, deletes nothing, reuses the class. ⚠️ First attempt read only page 1 and would have under-estimated the frontier (invoices sort by createdAt, and a prior run's far-future invoice has an OLD createdAt) — corrected to paginate. Proven: **three consecutive fees runs pass** at a stable ~15-19s, and the full Playwright suite is **42 passed / 2 skipped / 0 failed**.

Seed adds `admin@platform.pk` / `Admin!Secret12`. **UI** (`apps/web/app/admin/*`, separate from the tenant `(app)` group): `/admin/login`, `/admin/layout` (checks `GET /platform/auth/me`, own shell not the tenant sidebar), `/admin` tenants table (name/subdomain/plan/status/users/students + Suspend/Reactivate per row + New-tenant form). Platform writes use a dedicated `apps/web/lib/platform-api.ts` that reads the **`platform_csrf`** cookie (not the tenant `csrf`). integration e2e (provision → list → owner-login, dup 409, bad-subdomain 400, immediate revoke-on-disable, **refresh rotation + reuse-revokes-family + logout-revokes**) + a Playwright `admin.spec.ts` green. **Now at parity with tenant auth:** short 15m access token + **single-use rotating refresh** (`platform_refresh_tokens`, family-reuse detection; `/admin` client silently refreshes on 401); `platform_users`/`platform_refresh_tokens` revoked from `app_user`; per-request status re-check. **Remaining:** analytics/export, §22.9 break-glass support sessions. *(Monthly SMS-credit grants ✅ done via the `sms-monthly-credit` cron — M7.)*
- [x] **SuperAdmin Control Plane — SA0 (§24, §22.9), 2026-08-20.** The vendor console grows from one all-powerful operator into a real control plane. `PlatformUser.role` (`PlatformRole` = SUPER_ADMIN / SUPPORT / BILLING / ANALYST) splits **reads from writes**: every operator may list/read tenants, only **SUPER_ADMIN** may provision/suspend/reactivate (else 403); the tenant `PLATFORM_ADMIN` role is **removed** (a console operator is a `platform_users` row, never a tenant `Role`). Operators get their own **MFA** — `mfaEnabled`/`mfaSecretEnc` + a new `platform_mfa_recovery_codes` table — via `POST /platform/auth/mfa/enroll/begin|confirm` (TOTP enrolment → ten single-use recovery codes) and a two-step login (`{mfaRequired, mfaToken}` → `POST /platform/auth/mfa`, accepting a TOTP **or** a recovery code). Every write appends a **`platform_audit_logs`** row (action + `target_tenant_id` + acting `platform_user_id` + reason/ip), and **suspend now requires a reason** (blank → 400, and the reason is audited). Both new tables are non-tenant (added to `NON_TENANT_TABLES` in `scripts/check-rls-coverage.mjs`).
  - **Tests authored (this slice), against the locked SA0 contract:** `test/integration/platform-mfa.e2e-spec.ts` — enrol (begin→confirm with a TOTP off the returned secret) → ten recovery codes; a later login returns `{mfaRequired, mfaToken}` with no session; a valid TOTP completes the challenge and `/me` reflects `mfaEnabled:true`; a recovery code also completes it and is dead on reuse (single-use). `test/integration/platform-authz-audit.e2e-spec.ts` — an ANALYST reads the tenant list (200) but is 403 on provision/suspend/reactivate (and leaves no audit row); provision/suspend/reactivate each write one attributed `platform_audit_logs` row (`action`, `target_tenant_id`, acting `platform_user_id`, and the reason on suspend); a blank/missing suspend reason → 400. `platform.e2e-spec.ts` brought onto the new contract: the console admin is seeded `role: 'SUPER_ADMIN'`, the existing suspend sends a `reason`, and audit rows are cleared in teardown.
  - ⚠️ **Backend built in parallel — the specs are written to contract, not run green here** (the SA0 endpoints, `PlatformRole`, and the two tables are not in the working tree during authoring). They go green at integration once the backend lands. Assumed model accessors: `platform.platformAuditLog` / `platform.platformMfaRecoveryCode`; the blank-reason 400 is asserted as `VALIDATION_FAILED` (class-validator), and the two enrol endpoints are asserted 2xx-tolerant (200|201) since the contract does not lock their status.
  - ✅ **SA0 VERIFIED GREEN — 2026-08-25.** Run against the dev DB (localhost:5433) with the dev worker stopped: `prisma migrate status` clean (39/39 applied); `pnpm db:check-rls` passes all three gates including **vendor isolation** (`app_user` holds no privilege on any `platform_*` table — the 2026-08-24 grants hole stays closed); `tsc -p tsconfig.spec.json` exit 0; and the three platform specs run green — **3 suites / 32 tests / 0 fail** (`platform` · `platform-authz-audit` · `platform-mfa`). SA0 is now tested for *effect*, not only contract.
- [x] **SuperAdmin Control Plane — SA1 (Fleet Overview), 2026-08-25.** The vendor console gains the "record of everything" dashboard. A new **`platform_stats`** snapshot table (non-tenant — no `school_id`, on the `NON_TENANT_TABLES` allowlist AND revoked from `app_user` at the end of `06_grants.sql`, so `check-rls-coverage.mjs`'s vendor-isolation check now covers it automatically — `VENDOR_TABLES` derives from the `platform_*` prefix) is written nightly by a new worker job **`platform-stats-snapshot`** (BullMQ repeatable at 00:15; a handful of indexed cross-tenant COUNTs on the BYPASSRLS connection). `GET /platform/overview` (an **open read** — every operator role, like the tenant list, no `@PlatformRoles`) returns the latest snapshot: **schools** (total + active/suspended — deliberately **NO "trial" bucket**, that state isn't modelled, so none is invented), **students** (`student_enrollments` status ACTIVE — not raw `students`, which diverge), **staff** (`staff_profiles` with `left_at` null on a non-deleted user), and **new schools (30d)**. Metrics are DEFINED once server-side (SA-P7 / Law 4); the dashboard reads **O(one row)**, never a live fleet-wide COUNT (finding E). `/admin` renders a **Fleet overview** tile row above the tenants table (reuses `.metric`/`.badge`, Law 1 shape); an honest empty-state ("no snapshot yet — the nightly job will populate this") shows until the first run, and a dashboard fetch failure returns null rather than blanking the console. **Verified 2026-08-25** (dev DB, worker stopped for the run): migration applied (40/40); `db:check-rls` green incl. **vendor isolation now covering `platform_stats`**; `tsc -p tsconfig.spec.json` exit 0; **4 platform specs / 34 tests green** — new `platform-overview.e2e-spec.ts` (a read-only ANALYST reads the latest snapshot → 200; unauthenticated → 401, proving it is an open read) + SA0's three specs as clean regression. Seeded one real snapshot on dev (5 schools, 17 active students, 5 staff). ⚠️ **Adding the model tipped `tenant-prisma.service.ts` over TS's type-instantiation depth** (the known Prisma-depth fragility — a growing generated client) — fixed by splitting the `client` getter's `tx ?? xprisma` union so TS never structurally compares the extended client against `TransactionClient`; each branch casts through `unknown` (runtime identical). *(Remaining SuperAdmin phases: SA2 safe credential handoff — the plaintext `ownerPassword` is still live — then SA3–SA7.)*
- [x] **SuperAdmin Control Plane — SA2 credential handoff (SA-P3), 2026-08-25.** The plaintext `ownerPassword` is **removed from the console**, closing the one live thing that contradicted "no password ever typed into the console." `ProvisionTenantDto` drops the field entirely, so a sent password is **rejected server-side** (400 `VALIDATION_FAILED` via `forbidNonWhitelisted` — SA-P6, not merely a UI omission). Provisioning now creates the owner **INVITED** (the `ProvisioningService` already supported `passwordHash: null` + INVITED) and mints a **one-time onboarding token** reusing `password_reset_tokens` — written on the BYPASSRLS conn for the just-created tenant, **7-day TTL** (deliberately longer than the 30-min reset TTL: onboarding is delivered-then-acted, single-use + hashed so a wide window is safe). The API returns the raw token once (`{ id, subdomain, onboardingToken }`); the console builds the owner's **set-password link on their own tenant host** (`<subdomain>.<apex>/set-password?token=…` — the browser composes it, since the server can't know the public port — the URL gotcha) and shows it once with copy (mirrors the recovery-codes card). A new **public `/set-password` page** (`apps/web/app/set-password`) consumes the token via `POST /auth/reset-password` (which already sets the hash **and flips INVITED → ACTIVE** and clears lockout) then routes to owner-login; token read from `window.location` (not `useSearchParams`) to dodge Next 14's Suspense-boundary build failure. ⚠️ **Blast radius contained by the service seam:** `ProvisionInput.ownerPassword` stays optional, so all ~30 specs that call `provisionSchool(...)` directly (and the seed) are untouched — only the two HTTP-level platform specs sent a password, both updated. **Verified 2026-08-25** (worker stopped): backend+specs `tsc` exit 0, web `tsc` exit 0, **4 platform specs / 35 tests green** — `platform.e2e-spec` rewritten to the full flow (provision → onboarding token → `/auth/reset-password` on the tenant host → owner-login 200; owner is 401 before setting a password) plus a new "console refuses a typed password → 400" case. *(SA2's create-with-plan / custom-domain / edit-profile / trial-expired states deferred — they need SA3's plan model or new soft states; next up: SA3.)*
- [x] **SuperAdmin Control Plane — SA3a (plans & assignment), 2026-08-27.** `PlanTier` gains a real, server-side **entitlement catalog** — `PLAN_LIMITS[tier]` (`apps/api/src/modules/platform/plan-limits.ts`): maxStudents / maxStaff / maxCampuses / storageMb / monthlySmsCredits, **one source** (Law 4), keyed by tier, with monthlySmsCredits **referencing** the existing `PLAN_MONTHLY_SMS_CREDITS` (not duplicated). `GET /platform/plans` (open read) exposes it; `PATCH /platform/tenants/:id/plan` (SUPER_ADMIN, CSRF, audited **`TENANT_PLAN_CHANGE`** with from→to metadata) assigns / upgrades / downgrades. Console: the tenants table's Plan cell becomes a **per-row selector** for SUPER_ADMIN (other roles see text), and the Students cell shows **current / cap** from the catalog, with a footnote that caps are informational here. ⚠️ **SA3a assigns; it does NOT enforce** — the atomic count-vs-cap checks on the tenant request path (admission / staff / storage) are **SA3b**, deliberately separate because they touch several tenant modules and need a concurrency-safe count-then-write. **Verified 2026-08-27** (worker stopped): tsc (backend + web), lint, **5 platform specs / 40 tests** — new `platform-plans.e2e-spec` (catalog open-read 200; change-plan 200 + audit from→to; ANALYST 403 with no change; unknown tier → 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED`; missing-CSRF → 403). ⚠️ **Open decision for SA3b:** over-limit behaviour — hard-block the 501st admission vs. soft-allow-with-warning (a Pakistani school mid-fee-season may resent a hard block at the counter).
- [x] **SuperAdmin Control Plane — SA3b usage surfacing (soft-cap decided), 2026-08-27.** The over-limit decision is made as senior eng/UX: **soft-cap, not hard-block** — a school is never stopped from enrolling a child at the counter (UX for this market + the codebase's own "surface vs police" principle + avoiding the risky atomic count-in-the-admission-tx). The cap becomes a **visible overage signal** — the vendor's upsell lever. Built the surfacing: `listTenants` now returns **`activeStudents`** (ACTIVE enrollments — the SAME definition as the fleet dashboard, **Law 4**; replaced the raw `students` `_count` with a **filtered `_count: { enrollments: { where: { status: ACTIVE } } }`**); the console shows **current / cap** with **`at limit` / `over limit` badges** when a school reaches or exceeds its plan's `maxStudents`; and a new **Plans reference card** renders the catalog (what each tier grants) so the operator sees limits while assigning. **No tenant-path change** — admissions untouched. **Verified 2026-08-27:** tsc (backend + web), lint, 5 platform specs / 40 tests (platform.e2e now asserts the list carries `activeStudents`). ⚠️ **Deferred (opt-in, if ever wanted):** an actual tenant-path hard block, plus staff / campus / storage overage surfacing (the catalog already declares those caps).
- [x] **SuperAdmin Control Plane — SA4a (operator management), 2026-08-27.** Managing the vendor team is a SUPER_ADMIN function. `GET /platform/operators` (SUPER_ADMIN) lists operators — the select **never** returns `passwordHash` / `mfaSecretEnc`; `PATCH /platform/operators/:id` (SUPER_ADMIN, CSRF, audited **`OPERATOR_UPDATE`** with role/status from→to) changes an operator's **role** and/or **status** (enable/disable; DTO limits status to ACTIVE/DISABLED). ⚠️ **An operator can NEVER change their OWN role or status** (422) — which also protects the **last SUPER_ADMIN** for free (disabling/demoting X needs a *different* super-admin, so the last one is unreachable). Disabling takes effect on the target's next request (the guard re-checks status live — true since SA0). Console: a new **`/admin/operators`** page (SUPER_ADMIN-only, nav link gated) with a per-row role dropdown + enable/disable, own-row controls hidden. **Verified 2026-08-27:** tsc (backend + web), lint, **6 platform specs / 47 tests** — new `platform-operators.e2e` (list 200 no-secrets; ANALYST 403; role change 200 + audit from→to; self-change 422; disable 200; unknown 404). ⚠️ **Test-bug caught in verification** (not product): the spec first named operators `ops-superA-…` with an upper-case letter, but login looks up `email.toLowerCase()`, so the stored upper-case never matched → a 401 that read like bad credentials; fixed by lowercasing the fixtures. **A real note for SA4b:** the operator-create path must lowercase the email, exactly like provisioning does. **SA4b (next):** create a NEW operator via a secure one-time invite (a platform onboarding-token path mirroring SA2 — no password typed).
- [x] **SuperAdmin Control Plane — SA4b (operator invite & onboarding), 2026-08-27.** Creating a NEW operator with **no password typed** (SA-P3), mirroring SA2 on the platform side. `POST /platform/operators` (SUPER_ADMIN, CSRF, audited **`OPERATOR_CREATE`**) creates the operator **INVITED** with a **lowercased email** (or login's `email.toLowerCase()` lookup could never find them — the exact bug SA4a's verification caught) and mints a one-time onboarding token into a **new non-tenant `platform_password_reset_tokens`** table (allowlisted in `check-rls-coverage.mjs` + revoked from `app_user` in `06_grants.sql`, 7-day TTL); the console shows a **`/admin/set-password?token=…`** link once. `POST /platform/auth/set-password` (public) consumes the token → sets the hash, flips **INVITED → ACTIVE**, revokes stale refresh families. **Schema:** `PlatformUser.passwordHash` is now **nullable** (an INVITED operator has none), and the platform **login guards null passwordHash** as invalid-credentials (with the constant-time dummy hash, so it never reveals the invited-but-unset state). Console: a **New operator** form on `/admin/operators` (email/name/role) + the shown-once invite link; the admin layout now treats `/admin/set-password` as **public** so an INVITED operator isn't bounced to login. **Verified 2026-08-27** (worker stopped, client regenerated, migration applied): `db:check-rls` green incl. **vendor isolation covering `platform_password_reset_tokens`**; tsc (backend + web); lint; **7 platform specs / 54 tests** (new `platform-operator-create`); tenant-isolation suite 7/7. **SA4 is now complete** (manage + invite); an optional IP allowlist is the only listed extra, deferred.
- [x] **SuperAdmin Control Plane — SA5 (break-glass "login-as"), 2026-08-27.** A time-boxed, **read-only**, **RLS-scoped** vendor session into ONE school (SA-P8) — the system's highest-risk feature, built to the invariant that it rides the **normal tenant path, never BYPASSRLS**. `POST /platform/tenants/:id/break-glass` (SUPER_ADMIN **or** SUPPORT, CSRF, reason required, audited **`BREAK_GLASS_START`**) issues a 30-min tenant access token scoped to the target school (`TokenService.signBreakGlass` → `{ sid: targetSchool, roles: [OWNER_ADMIN], bg: true, vop }`). Because it is a *normal* access token, it is confined to that one school by **TenantScopeGuard** (replayed on any other host → 403 `TENANT_MISMATCH`) + **RLS** (GUC-scoped) — physically incapable of reaching a second tenant. `POST /auth/break-glass-enter` (public) sets the read-only session cookie on the school's own host; a new `/break-glass` web page (token in the URL **fragment**, kept out of logs/Referer) enters and lands on `/dashboard`. **Read-only** enforced by a new **`BreakGlassReadonlyGuard`** (non-GET + `req.user.breakGlass` → 403) registered after `JwtAuthGuard`, plus no csrf cookie (belt-and-braces). `RequestUser`/`AccessClaims` gained `breakGlass`/`vendorOperatorId`; `JwtAuthGuard` attaches them; `/auth/me` returns a synthetic read-only "me" so the shell loads (a break-glass session has no tenant user row). Console: an **Enter** action per tenant row (SUPER_ADMIN + SUPPORT). ⚠️ **D5 not needed for v1:** a read-only session writes no tenant rows, so no `audit_logs`-actor schema change — the entry is recorded in `platform_audit_logs`; owner-notification (D4) + a WRITE-capable break-glass (which WOULD need the D5 nullable-actor `audit_logs` change) are deferred. **Verified 2026-08-27:** tsc (backend + web), lint, **8 platform specs / 60 tests** (new `platform-break-glass`: start+audit; SUPPORT 200 / ANALYST 403; reason 400; scoped read 200; **confinement 403 `TENANT_MISMATCH`**; **read-only write 403**), **tenant-isolation suite 7/7** (the core-auth changes didn't weaken isolation). **LIVE-verified on :4000** with a minted token: enter 204 · scoped read 200 · confinement 403 · read-only write 403.

## 🔁 How to update this file (every phase)
1. Move the milestone row to ✅ with the ship date; set the next one to ⏳.
2. Fill its checklist, list the tests that went green, and note any findings/deviations.
3. Update the header (`updated`, `current_milestone`, test count) and the top gates line.
4. Update any brain note whose **Implementation status** changed, and [[Roadmap & Milestones]] if sequencing shifted.
5. Commit with the phase (the repo history + this file must agree).
